<page-num>121</page-num>

# LIB. IX. Of the Nature of things.

...just seven were not present at their birth, this matter cannot be referred to here.

And even if it should be that a man were born with seven fingers, whether on one hand or on both, that would be a monstrosity and outside the usual order of Nature, and not subject to the influence of the stars. Therefore, this also cannot belong to this discussion.

But if there must be only five fingers on each hand, yet there are seven planets, and if these are to be compared with one another, it would then be necessary for the names of the planets to be put into a box to draw lots, to determine which two planets must step aside and be left out.

Yet because this cannot be, and the planets have neither dice nor lots in the Firmament, it is astonishing that anyone should take this power upon himself to assign by the name and sign of the planet: the thumb to *Venus*, the forefinger to *Jupiter*, the middle finger to *Saturn*, the ring finger to the *Sun*, and the little finger to *Mercury*—while in the meantime casting out *Mars* and the *Moon*, as if they were excluded from their own tribe and freedom.

Since things are this way, who can wonder if *Mars* stirs up his sons <note>Those individuals born under the astrological influence of the planet Mars.</note> with righteous indignation to kill that drawer of lots, or to bear a perpetual hatred against him? Who can wonder why the Moon should weaken the brain of that gambler, or take away his reason?

And this is their first error which, as we have said, they have committed in <term>chiromancy</term> <note>original: "Chiromancy"; the practice of palm reading or divination by the hand.</note>.

->The second error in it is this:<-

### Of the Nature of Things. Book 9.

It often happens that the original and natural lines of the hands are changed by injuries and accidents, or are made larger or smaller, or appear in different places.

Just as if a highway were obstructed by something—whether blocked by a mountain landslide or ruined by a flood—people find another path nearby. The same thing happens with the old lines of the hands: sometimes, after the healing of wounds or ulcers, new lines grow along with the new flesh, and the old ones are completely removed.

In the same way, hard labor can wear away these lines, or the original lines may grow larger. It is the same with trees: if a young tree puts out many branches on all sides and these are pruned, the trunk of the tree itself becomes larger.

But so that we may at length proceed to the practice of the art of *chiromancy* <term>palmistry; the art of divination by the lines and features of the hands</term> and briefly state our opinion: I want you to know that I will not change anything regarding the hands, but will instead accept and be satisfied with the observations and descriptions of the ancient authors. However, in this practice of *chiromancy*, I intend to write about those things which the ancients never mentioned, such as the *chiromancy* of *herbs*, *woods*, *stones*, and similar things.

<margin>The *chiromancy* of herbs and woods.</margin>
It must first be noted that all herbs of the same species must share the same *chiromancy*.

But if their lines are different, appearing larger or smaller in some specimens, that is because of 
->their<-

<header>BOOK VIII. Of the Nature of Things.</header>

<margin>What use they have.</margin>
...their age. We do therefore clearly profess that the <term>Chiromancy of Herbs</term> <note>original: "Chiromancy of Hearbs"; the practice of reading the lines or signs on plants to determine their properties or age, similar to palmistry</note> contributes to nothing else but to know and understand the age of any herb or root.

But by way of argument, someone may object here that no herb is older than another as long as it grows—four or five months at the most—if the calculation <note>original: "supputation"</note> begins from May until autumn, at which time herbs die and fall from their roots.

<margin>The condition of the spirit of the herb.</margin>
To this I answer that from God there is but one power <note>original: "vertue"</note> in a root, which is the primary essence and spirit of the herb. Because of this spirit, the herb grows and is supported until its predestined time, and until it is elevated to the production of seed. This is a sign and a mark that the power returns back to the root, and so the herb dries up. As long as that spirit, which is the chief power of the herb, remains in the root, the herb is renewed every year—unless it happens that the spirit is taken away along with the herb and decays. Then the herb is not renewed, for the root is dead and has no more life remaining in it.

But the manner in which that spirit is taken from the root along with the herb, or from the earth along with the root—so that its power cannot return to the root or from the root into the earth—is not to be considered in this place. For that is a sublime mystery of Nature and is not to be openly revealed because of ignorant physicians <note>original: "Physitians"</note>, who not only scoff at but despise <note>original: "contemne"</note> such secrets. Therefore, what we have omitted here, we shall set down in our Herbal <note>A book describing the names and medicinal properties of plants</note>.

Moreover, the younger the herbs are, the more they excel in strength and power.

<header>*Of the Nature of Things.* BOOK IX.</header>

Just as a man is weakened and fails in strength by age, so also do herbs.

But to know what the *chiromancy* <note>original: "Chiromancy"; here referring to the study of physical signs, lines, or "signatures" to determine a plant's age and properties</note> and age of herbs and such things are, daily experience is required. Since the number of their years is not written upon them, it must be discovered, as I have said, by *chiromancy* alone.

Now *chiromancy* does not consider numbers, letters, or characters, but only lines, veins, and wrinkles, according to the age of any thing. For the older a thing is, the larger and easier to see are its lines; meanwhile, the virtue <note>original: "vertue"; the inherent power or medicinal efficacy of a substance</note> and operation of the thing become much duller.

<margin>Young herbs must be applied to old diseases, and vice versa.</margin>
For just as a disease of one month or year is more easily cured than one that is two, three, four, or five months or years old, so a herb cures a disease of one month or year sooner than one that is two, three, or four months or years old. For this reason, young herbs must be applied to old ailments <note>original: "griefs"</note>, and old herbs or medicines to young or new diseases. For if an old herb were applied to an old disease, the blind would lead the blind, and both would fall into the ditch.

This is the reason why many medicines do not work but are taken into the body and occupy the limbs <note>original: "members"</note> like dirt sticks to shoes; consequently, diseases are afterwards doubled.

Now ignorant physicians never considered this, but through their ignorance have destroyed more people than they ever cured.

Therefore, first of all, you who are physicians must know that the medicine must be younger than the disease, so that it may be stronger to expel it. For

---

If the medicine is more powerful than the disease, the disease will be overcome as fire is quenched with water. But if the disease is stronger than the medicine, it turns the medicine into poison, causing the diseases to be doubled and increased afterward.

So if a disease is like iron, it must be cured with a <term>chalybeate</term> <note>original: "Chalybeat"; refers to medicines containing iron or steel, named after the Chalybes, an ancient people famous for blacksmithing</note> medicine. For steel cannot be overcome by iron. The more powerful always overcomes, and the weaker is overcome.

Although it was not our original purpose to write about <term>medicine</term> <note>original: "Physick"</note> in this place, yet for the sake of true and genuine <term>physicians</term>, I could not pass over these things in silence.

# Of Mineral Signs

<margin>How minerals differ.</margin>
**M**inerals and metals also have their true signs and meanings before they are exposed to fire. They have received these from the <term>Archeus</term> <note>original: "Archeius"; in the philosophy of Paracelsus, this is the "internal chemist" or vital force that directs the growth and maturation of all things in nature</note> and the superior stars, with each one resembling its kind through distinct colors and differences in the earth. For the mineral of gold is distinct from the mineral of silver; likewise, the mineral of silver is distinct from that of copper, the mineral of copper from that of iron, the mineral of iron from tin and lead, and so on for the rest.

No one can deny that the minerals and all metallic bodies in mines, which lie hidden in the earth,

<header>*Of the Nature of things.* BOOK IX.</header>

...may be known by the art of *chiromancy* <note>original: "Chiromancy"; usually referring to palmistry, Paracelsus here uses it to mean the study of external physical signs that reveal internal qualities</note> by their exterior signs. That is, the *chiromancy* of mines, veins, and conduits, etc., by which not only those things hidden within them are brought forth, but also a certain depth, richness of the mine, and plenty of metal is revealed. And in this *chiromancy*, three things are necessary to be known: namely, the age, depth, and breadth of the veins, as was said a little before regarding herbs. For the older the veins are, the richer and more enlarged the mines become.

Concerning this, we must know that all metals that still lie hidden in their *matrix* <note>original: "matrix"; the natural environment or "womb" in which a mineral or fossil is embedded and grows</note> grow constantly.

From this, it is also manifest that everything that grows—even if it is removed from its *matrix*—cannot be made smaller, but immediately grows (that is, it is multiplied). According to its substance, measure, and weight, it grows until its predestinated time. Now, this predestinated time is the third part of the appointed age of all minerals, vegetables, and animals, <margin>There are three chief categories <note>original: "heads"</note> of all things.</margin> which are the three chief categories of all earthly things.

Now, whatever still remains in its *matrix* grows until the *matrix* dies. For the *matrix* has its appointed time to live and die, especially if it is subject to external elements.

<margin>What the limit <note>original: "tearme"</note> of the elements is.</margin>
That which is not subject to them has no other time or limit than the elements themselves have, with which it shall also die and perish on the day of *Renovation* <note>original: "Renovation"; the theological or alchemical concept of the final renewal of the world</note> (which is their limit).

Hence it follows that all things within the earth are not subjected to the external elements, nei-

<header>BOOK IX. Of the Nature of things.</header>

Neither are they sensitive to cold, moisture, dryness, wind, or air, by which they might be destroyed. Such kinds of bodies, therefore, cannot rot, or contract any filthiness, or stink, or die, as long as they continue within the earth in their *Chaos* <note>original: "Chaos"; in Paracelsian alchemy, this refers to the primordial state of matter or the specific atmosphere within the earth that sustains minerals</note>.

So much has been said of metals; and just as it is with them, so also it may be said of many men who live for some hundreds of years in the caves of mountains—such as giants or pygmies—about whom we have written a specific book.

But to descend to the practice of the <term>chiromancy</term> <note>the art of "reading" the signs and lines of the earth, much like palmistry reads a hand</note> of <term>mines</term>, which we shall explain to you in few words: you must know that the deeper and broader the veins are, the older they are. For where the course of the veins extends a great distance and at length diminishes and is not hidden, it is a bad sign. For as the courses of the veins diminish, so also do the <term>mines</term> themselves diminish, which is shown by their depth. Although sometimes good <term>mines</term> are found, the deeper they go, the more they often diminish, so that it is not worth the effort to work in them. But when the veins are enlarged with other additions, or are frequently cut off, it is a good sign, showing that the <term>mines</term> are not only good at the top, but that they are also increased and multiplied in depth and length for the most part; the <term>mines</term> being made richer, they yield pure gold, and thus a very large treasure.

It is unfounded that many miners praise only those veins which go directly downward and incline from the *East* to the *West*. For the nature of the

# Of the Nature of Things. Book IX.

Mining and experience teach that veins which stretch from the West to the East, or from the South to the North—and likewise from the North to the South—are no less rich than others. Therefore, no vein should be valued more than another based solely on its direction. However, we think it appropriate to discuss this no further.

<margin>By what signs mines are discovered.</margin>
Now, regarding the other signs of the internal earth, as well as the colors of minerals, we shall briefly discuss them as follows.

Whenever miners encounter "fat" earth <note>original: "fat earth"; likely referring to rich, oily, or clay-like soil that suggests the presence of minerals</note>, which indicates a vein of pure and new metal, that is a very good sign ensuring that the metal of that vein is not far off.

Similarly, if the earth that is dug up has no metal in it but is "fat," or of a white, black, green, or blue color, or like clay, etc., then that is also a good sign of some valuable metal hidden beneath it. Therefore, you must continue digging and not give up.

Miners primarily pay attention to the most excellent, beautiful, and prominent colors, such as green earth, or <term>chrysocolla</term> <note>original: "Chrysocoll"; a green copper silicate mineral</note>, <term>verdigris</term> <note>original: "Verdegrease"; a green pigment or crust formed on copper</note>, azure, <term>cinnabar</term> <note>original: "Cinnabar"; mercury sulfide, used as a red pigment</note>, <term>sandarac</term> <note>original: "Sandaracha"; here likely referring to red arsenic sulfide or realgar</note>, <term>orpiment</term> <note>original: "Auripigmentum"; a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>, <term>litharge of gold</term> <note>original: "Litharge of Gold"; a secondary mineral form of lead monoxide produced during the refining of precious metals</note>, and <term>silver</term>, etc. Almost every one of these usually signifies a specific metal or mineral.

Thus, <term>verdigris</term>, <term>chrysocolla</term>, and green earth mostly signify <term>copper</term>.

Likewise, azure, white <term>arsenic</term>, or <term>litharge of silver</term> signify the metal <term>copper</term>.

Similarly, <term>cinnabar</term> and <term>sandarac</term> sometimes signify <term>gold</term>, sometimes <term>silver</term>, and sometimes a mixture of both.

# Book IX. Of the Nature of Things.

So orpiment <note>original: Auripigmentum</note>, red sulfur, and litharge of gold mostly signify the presence of gold.

When chrysocolla <note>original: Chrysocoll</note> is found mixed with azurite <note>original: Azure</note>, or azurite with chrysocolla and orpiment <note>original: Anripigment</note>, they usually signify an excellent and rich mineral deposit.

Where stones or earth are found of an iron-like color, they certainly indicate an iron mine.

You should note that it sometimes happens that the <term>Archeus</term> <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, the vital force or "internal chemist" believed to govern natural processes</note> of the earth casts up some metal from the deeper parts of the earth through secret passages. That is a most encouraging sign.

Miners, therefore, must not be discouraged when they see such certain signs and the hope of an excellent mine lying beneath. If thin leaves of metals, like <term>talc</term> <note>likely referring to mica or other flaky, shimmering minerals</note>, cling to rocks or stones, that is a most certain sign.

<margin>What shimmering lights signify in mines.</margin>
Regarding <term>coruscations</term> <note>shimmering lights or flashes believed by early miners to appear above ore deposits</note>, they must be diligently and studiously observed, for they are the most certain signs of metals lying hidden beneath them; they also indicate the extent and type of those metals. However, we must note that those metals have not yet reached perfection but are still in their initial state of being. Wherever the shimmering light reaches, the vein of metal reaches just as far.

<margin>Shimmering lights appear in three colors.</margin>
Moreover, you must know that these shimmering lights come in three colors: white, yellow, and red, by which all metals are revealed to us. A white light signifies white metals, such as tin, lead, and silver; a red light signifies red metals, such as copper and iron; and a yellow light signifies golden metals.

Furthermore, a thin and subtle coruscation <note>original: "Coruſcation"; a flashing or sparkling of light thought by early miners and alchemists to indicate the presence of ore</note> is the best sign.

For just as you see with trees, the fewer flowers there are, the better, larger, and more flavorful the fruit will be; similarly, small and subtle flashes signify fine and excellent metals, while the opposite is true for larger flashes.

Moreover, you must know that as long as those flashes appear—whether they are great or small, or of this or that color—the metals in those mines have not yet reached perfection. They are still in their primary state <note>original: "first being"</note>, like a man's sperm in a woman's womb <note>original: "matrix"</note>.

<margin>What coruscation is.</margin>
But now we must consider here what coruscation is. We should know that it appears in the mines by night like a sparkling fire, just like gunpowder that is laid in a long line <note>original: "train"</note> and, being lit at one end, creates a long flash.

In the same way, the flash travels from the east to the west, or from the west to the east, from the south to the north, or the reverse.

All these flashes, however they appear, are a certain sign of the veins <note>original: "Courses"</note> of metals, so that they may be recognized, and metals—as certain gifts from God—may be brought out of the earth. For whatever God created for man’s use, He placed that nature into it so that it would not remain hidden. And although it was hidden, He created specific external signs to aid in finding it, by which its wonderful purpose <note>original: "predestination"</note> may be known.

In the same way, if men hide treasures, they mark the place with certain signs and bury...

<header>BOOK IX. Of the Nature of things.</header>

<margin>How the Chaldeans and Greeks hide their treasure.</margin>
The ancient *Chaldeans* and *Greeks*, fearing they would be driven away or banished in times of war, would hide their treasure. They would mark the place by choosing a certain day, hour, and minute of the year, observing where the Sun or Moon cast a shadow, and there they would bury and hide their treasure.

<margin>What Sciomancy is.</margin>
They call this art <term>*Sciomancy*</term> <note>original: "Sciomancy, i. e. the Art of Shadowing"</note>, which is the art of using shadows. Many arts have been founded upon these shadowings, many hidden things have been revealed through them, and all spirits and astral bodies are known by this method.

These are Kabbalistic signs that cannot deceive, and therefore they should be diligently observed.

<margin>Divining rods are unreliable.</margin>
You must therefore take care that you do not allow yourselves to be seduced by the divinations of uncertain arts, for they are vain and fruitless—especially divining rods, which have deceived many miners. For if they show a thing truly once, they fail ten times.

We also must not trust other fraudulent signs of the devil, which are made and appear preternaturally at night and at inconvenient times, such as ghosts and visions. I would have you know that the devil can show and cause signs, but only out of pure fraud and deceit.

Just as there is no church built where the devil does not also have his chapel, and no chapel built where he does not set up his altar, there is no good seed that he does not sow

# Of the Nature of Things: Book IX

...amongst it his tares <note>a reference to the Parable of the Tares (Matthew 13:24–30), where an enemy sows weeds among wheat; here it refers to the devil's deception</note>. The same is the nature of visions and supernatural apparitions in crystals, beryls <note>original: "Berills"; transparent gemstones often used as "show-stones" for divination</note>, looking-glasses, and waters. These are basely abused by ceremonial necromancers <note>original: "Negromancers"; practitioners who conjure spirits or the dead</note>, contrary to God’s command and the power of the light of Nature.

Visions, indeed, are not to be absolutely rejected, for they have their place; however, they must be achieved through a different process. We are no longer in the "first generation," but the "second." Therefore, ceremonies and conjurations are no longer to be used by us Christians in the state of regeneration <note>spiritual rebirth or the New Covenant</note>, as the ancients in the Old Testament used them while living in the first generation. For those prefigurations <note>symbolic events or types that foreshadowed later truths</note> were meant for us who were to live under the New Testament.

Whatever things the ancients—who were under the Old Testament and in the first generation—did by ceremonies and conjurations, we Christians of the second generation and the New Testament must do through prayer, knocking, and seeking, and must obtain through faith.

points the

In these three chief points consists the entire foundation of the magical and Kabbalistic <note>original: "Cabalisticall"; referring to Christian Kabbalah, a mystical system used to understand the divine</note> art. By these, we may obtain whatever we desire, so that for us Christians, nothing is impossible.

But we shall stop treating further of these and other monuments of Kabbalah here, as they are discussed sufficiently in the book *Of Visions*. I refer you to that work, so that you may see how wonderfully Christ, the Son of God, works through His angels in us Christians and the faithful, and how brotherly He converses with us. From this, we are the true angels, and...

<header>BOOK IX. Of the Nature of Things.</header>

members of Christ, as he is our head, as he is in us, so we live in him, as is taught in the book of the Lord's Supper.

<margin>What is the power <note>original: "vertue"</note> of the Philosopher's Tincture.</margin>
But to return to our purpose concerning mineral signs, and especially concerning the flashing <note>original: "Coruscation"</note> of metal <note>original: "Metalline"</note> veins, we must know that just as metals which are still in their original state <note>original: "first being"</note> send out their flashing—that is, signs—so also the Philosopher's Tincture <note>The legendary substance, also known as the Philosopher's Stone, capable of transmuting base metals into gold or silver.</note>, which changes all imperfect metals into silver and gold (changing white metals into silver and red into gold), puts forth its own signs like a flash if it is celestially <note>original: "Astrally"</note> perfected and prepared. For as soon as a small quantity of it is cast upon a molten <note>original: "fluxil"</note> metal so that they mix together in the fire, there arises a natural flashing and brightness, similar to that of fine gold or silver in a refining cup <note>original: "test"</note>. This is then a sign that the gold or silver is freed and purified <note>original: "purged"</note> without the addition of any other metals.

<margin>How the Philosopher's Tincture is made celestial <note>original: "astrall"</note>.</margin>
But how the Philosopher's Tincture is made celestial <note>original: "Astral"</note>, you must understand it in this way:

First of all, you must know that every metal, as long as it lies hidden in its original state <note>original: "first being"</note>, has its certain peculiar stars.

So gold has the stars of the Sun, silver the stars of the Moon, copper the stars of Venus, iron the stars of Mars, tin the stars of Jupiter, lead the stars of Saturn, and quicksilver <term>mercury</term> the stars of Mercury.

But as soon as they reach their perfection and are solidified <note>original: "coagulated"</note> into a fixed metallic body, their stars fall away and leave them, like a dead body.

Hence it follows that all such bodies are afterwards dead and ineffective, and that the unconquered star <note>In alchemy, the "star" refers to the essential spiritual essence or "virtue" of a substance.</note> of *metals* overcomes them all, converts them into its nature, and makes them all *astral* <note>original: "Astrall"; refers to a substance purified to its celestial or spiritual essence.</note>.

<margin>*Gold* that is made by the *tincture* is better than natural gold.</margin>
For this reason, our *gold* and *silver*—which are colored <note>original: "tinged"</note> and prepared with our *tincture*—are much more excellent and better for the preparation of *medicinal* secrets than that which is natural, which *nature* generates in the *mines* and is afterwards separated from other *metals*.

So also the *mercury* of a body is made *astrally* from another body, and is much more noble and stable <note>original: "fixt"</note> than common *mercury*. The same applies to the other *metals*.

I say, therefore, that every *alchemist* who has that star of *gold* can turn all *red metals* <note>Likely referring to copper or its alloys.</note> into *gold* by transforming <note>original: "tinging"</note> them.

So, by the star of *silver*, all *white metals* are changed into *silver*; by the star of *copper* into *copper*; by the star of *quicksilver* into *mercury* of the body, and so with the rest.

But how all these stars are prepared according to the *spagyrical art* <note>original: "Spagiricall Art"; the Paracelsian method of alchemy involving the separation and recombination of natural elements.</note> is not our purpose to declare at this time. Instead, their explanation belongs to our books on the *Transmutation of Metals*.

<margin>The nature of the *red tincture*.</margin>
But as for what concerns their signs, I would have you know that our *red tincture* (which contains the stars of *gold*) is of a most stable <note>original: "fixt"</note> substance, of the quickest penetration, and of a most intense redness. In powder, it resembles the color of *saffron*, but in its whole body, it is the color of a *ruby*. I say it is a *tincture* as easily melted <note>original: "fluxil"</note> as *wax*, and as transparent as

<margin>The star of iron.</margin>
The <term>star</term> of <term>iron</term> is very red and as transparent as a <term>garnet</term> <note>original: Granate</note>. It is as <term>fluid</term> <note>original: fluxil</note> as <term>resin</term>, as brittle as <term>glass</term>, consists of a fixed substance, and is much heavier than its base metal.

It is as <term>fluid</term> <note>original: fluxile</note> as <term>resin</term>, as brittle as <term>glass</term>, and equal to gold in weight, making it heavier than ordinary <term>lead</term>.

<margin>The star of quicksilver.</margin>
The <term>star</term> of <term>quicksilver</term> <note>mercury</note> has a wonderful, sparkling white color, like snow in the coldest weather. It is very subtle and has a penetrating, corrosive sharpness. It is as transparent as <term>crystal</term>, flows as easily as <term>resin</term>, and is very cold to the taste but very hot within—as if it were fire—though it is a very <term>volatile</term> substance when exposed to fire.

By this description, the stars of the metals are to be known and understood.

<header>Of the Nature of Things. BOOK IX.</header>
<meta>The previous page discussed the preparation of substances and the importance of understanding the "Three Principles" (Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt) in alchemy. This page continues instructions on identifying the composition of metals and organic materials through their reactions to fire.</meta>

You must not, at the beginning of your work, take the physical body of gold or silver, but rather the **first being** <note>original: "first being"; referring to the *prima materia* or the essential, unrefined state of the metal</note> of gold and silver. For if an error is committed at the start, all your effort and labor will be in vain.

You must also understand regarding metals that every one of them receives a unique sign in the fire by which it is known. These signs include sparks, flames, glimmers, and the color, smell, and taste of the fire, and so on.

For instance, the true sign of gold or silver in the **test** <term>the cupellation process used to refine and assay precious metals</term> is a shimmering flash. When that appears, it is certain that lead and other metals that were mixed in have evaporated <note>original: "fumed away"</note>, and that the gold and silver are fully purified.

The signs of iron being red-hot in the furnace are clear, transparent sparks flying upward. When those appear, the iron will be burned like straw unless it is removed from the fire.

<margin>How to know if metals have more or less of the Three Principles.</margin>
In the same way, any earthly body shows its unique and distinct signs in the fire, revealing whether it has more Mercury, Sulfur, or Salt, and which of the three principles it contains the most. For if it produces smoke <note>original: "fume"</note> before it flames, it is a sign that it contains more Mercury than Sulfur.

But if it immediately burns with a flame and without any smoke, it is a sign that it contains much Sulfur and little or no Mercury.

You see this in fatty substances such as tallow, oil, resin, and the like. However, if it turns entirely into smoke without any flame, it is a sign that there is much Mercury and little or no Sulfur in it. This happens in herbs, flowers, and other plant <note>original: "Vegetable"</note> substances and volatile bodies.

## *Of some specific Signs of Natural and Supernatural things.*

**We** must speak further of some specific signs, about which we have said nothing until now. In this treatise, it will be very necessary for you—who boast of having skill in the **Art of Signatures** <note>The "Doctrine of Signatures" was the belief that the physical appearance of a plant, mineral, or object revealed its hidden medicinal or spiritual purpose.</note> and who desire to be called **Signators**—to understand me correctly. For in this place, we shall not write theoretically but practically, and shall declare our opinion in a few words.

<margin>What the Art of Signing is</margin>
And first, know that the Art of Signing teaches how true and suitable names are to be given to all things, all of which **Adam** truly knew. For immediately after the Creation, he gave to everything its proper name: to animals, trees, herbs, roots, stones, minerals, metals, waters, etc. And to all the fruits of the earth, water, air, fire, and so on. The names he gave them all were ratified and approved by God. For from the true and intrinsic—

<page-num>138</page-num>

# Of the Nature of Things. Book 9.
<note>original: "LIB. IX."</note>

...he took them all as his foundation; not from personal opinion, or from a predetermined science—namely, the Art of Signing <note>The "Art of Signing" refers to the Doctrine of Signatures, the belief that every object in nature has a physical "sign" indicating its purpose or character.</note>.

<margin>Adam was the first signator <note>signator: one who assigns names or signs based on nature</note>.</margin>
**Adam**, therefore, was the first signator. It cannot be denied that true and proper names also flow from the **Hebrew** language, and are given to every thing according to its nature and condition.

<margin>The nature of the Hebrew language.</margin>
For the names given to things in the Hebrew language simultaneously signify their virtue <note>original: "vertue"; here meaning inherent power or medicinal quality</note>, power, and properties.

So, when we say this is a **hog**, a **horse**, a **cow**, a **bear**, a **dog**, a **fox**, or a **sheep**:

<margin>The meaning of a hog, horse, cow, bear, fox, dog, and sheep in the Hebrew language.</margin>
The name of a **hog** signifies a filthy and unclean animal; a **horse** signifies a strong and patient animal; a **cow**, a devouring and insatiable beast; a **bear**, a strong, victorious, and untamed brute; a **fox**, a shifty and crafty beast; a **dog**, an animal false even to those of its own kind; and a **sheep**, a mild and profitable beast that is harmful to none.

Thus, a man is called a **hog** because of his sordid and swinish life.

And he is called a **horse** for his endurance <note>original: "bearing"</note>, for which he is distinguished.

And a **cow**, because he is insatiable with food and drink, and knows no limit to his appetite.

And a **bear**, because he is misshapen and stronger than other men.

A **fox**, because he is a turncoat and deceitful, adapting himself to everyone while offending no one.

A **dog**, because he is faithful to no one but his own mouth, and is false and unhelpful <term>inofficious: failing in duty or kindness to others</term> to all.

And a **sheep**, because he hurts no one but himself, and is useful to everyone else rather than himself, and so on.

<margin>In what herbs there is a signature.</margin>
In this way also, many herbs and roots have received their names.

So <term>Eyebright</term> <note>original: "Eye-bright"; a plant traditionally used in folk medicine to treat eye infections and redness</note> is so called because it cures weak and sore eyes.

So the root <term>Bloodwort</term> <note>original: "Bloudwort"</note> is so called because it stops bleeding better than any other.

So the herb <term>Pilewort</term> <note>also known as Lesser Celandine; used historically to treat hemorrhoids (piles)</note> is so called because it cures the piles better than other herbs.

The same may also be said of various other herbs, of which I could name a great number; all these were so named because of their virtue and power, as shall be more fully declared in our <term>Herbal</term> <note>original: "Herball"; a reference to a book describing the names and properties of plants</note>.

Moreover, there are many herbs and roots which are named not only for their innate virtue and power, but also for their figure, form, and appearance. These include <term>Devil's-bit</term>, <term>Five-leaved grass</term> (or <term>Cinquefoil</term>), <term>Hound’s-tongue</term>, <term>Adder’s-tongue</term>, <term>Horsetail</term>, <term>Liverwort</term>, <term>Ox-tongue</term>, <term>Lungwort</term>, the herb <term>Chameleon</term>, <term>St. John’s wort</term> (also called "the herb bored through" <note>referring to the tiny translucent spots on its leaves that look like holes</note>), the herb <term>Dog-stone</term> <note>a type of orchid, named for the shape of its roots</note>, <term>Tongue-laurel</term>, <term>Thorough-leaf</term>, <term>Turnsole</term> <note>a plant that turns its flowers to follow the sun</note>, and many others which shall be considered individually, not here, but in the <term>Herbal</term>.

<margin>What signature there is in animals.</margin>
The same also holds true concerning the signs of animals; for by the blood and its circle <note>referring to the patterns or "corona" observed in samples during diagnosis</note>, and by the urine and its circle, all diseases that lie hidden in a person may be known.

By the liver of a slaughtered beast, it is known whether the flesh is healthy to be eaten or not. For unless the liver is clear and of a red color—rather than blue, yellow, rough, or full of holes—the beast is diseased, and therefore its meat is unwholesome.

### Of the Nature of Things. Book IX.

<margin>The liver is the origin of blood.</margin>
And it is no wonder that the liver can show that by natural signs. For the origin of the blood is in the liver, and from there it is diffused through the veins into the whole body and is solidified <note>original: "coagulated"</note> into flesh.

Therefore, from a diseased and poorly functioning liver, no healthy or fresh blood can be produced, just as no wholesome flesh can be formed from bad blood.

Also, even without looking at the liver, the state of the flesh and blood may be known. For if both are healthy, they have their true and natural color, which is red and clear, mixed with no other unusual color, such as yellow or blue. For those unusual colors signify diseases and sicknesses.

<margin>What the knots in an infant's navel signify.</margin>
There are also other signs worthy of admiration: namely, when the <term>Archeius</term> <note>In Paracelsian medicine, the "Archeius" is the internal "alchemist" or vital force that governs the body's growth and health.</note> acts as the <term>Signator</term> <note>The "Signer" or natural force that imprints physical marks (signatures) to reveal an object's purpose or history.</note> and marks the navel of an infant with little knots. By these, it may be guessed how many children the mother has already brought forth or shall bring forth in the future.

<margin>The branches of a stag's antlers signify his age.</margin>
The same <term>Signator</term> marks the horns of a hart <note>A male deer, specifically a Red Deer stag.</note> with branches, by which his age is known. For as many branches as the antler has, that is how many years old the hart is. And since he grows a new antler every year, the age of the hart may be known up to twenty or thirty years.

<margin>What the circles on a cow's horns mean.</margin>
In the same way, the <term>Signator</term> of a cow marks her horns with circles, by which it may be known how many calves she has produced. For every circle signifies one calf.

<margin>The teeth of a horse.</margin>
The same <term>Signator</term> brings forth the first teeth of a horse, so that for the first seven years his age may be known by his teeth. For at first, a horse is born with fourteen teeth, of which he loses two every year, and so in the space of seven years, he loses them all.

<margin>Colours of the circle of the Moon.</margin>

Redness signifies that there will be wind; greenness and blackness signify rain; these two mixed together signify both wind and rain. The same signs in the sky above the sea are a sign of great storms and tempests. Clarity and a bright whiteness is a good sign, especially on the ocean, for it usually signifies a quiet, fair season.

Whatever the moon portends <term>portends</term><note>foreshadows or predicts</note> by her signs shall come to pass the next day.

Or, as one who overcomes the keeper of a house casts him out by force and sets himself as master of the house, disposing of all things according to his pleasure and making the other his servant.

<margin>What things are subject to the Sun.</margin>

<margin>What things are subject to Jupiter.</margin>

<margin>What things are subject to Venus.</margin>

<margin>What things are subject to Saturn.</margin>

...or noise, and so on, in the mines by <term>flashes of light</term> <note>original: "Coruscations"</note>: in the sky <note>original: "Firmament"</note> by stars, comets, flashing, and lightning, and <term>star-jelly</term> <note>original: "Nostock"; a gelatinous substance traditionally thought to be the remains of fallen stars</note>, and the like: in visions by <term>salamander-like</term> <note>original: "Salamandrine"</note> and fiery spirits.

<margin>What the signs of Hydromancy are.</margin>
<term>Hydromancy</term> <note>divination by water</note> gives its signs through the "stars of the water," such as by floods, droughts <note>original: "scarcity"</note>, changes in water color, turbulent motions, new streams, and the washing away of earthy things. In magic and <term>Necromancy</term> <note>divination by the dead</note>, signs appear through nymphs, visions, and supernatural monsters in the rivers and the sea.

<margin>What the signs of Chaomancy are.</margin>
<term>Chaomancy</term> <note>divination by the air</note> shows its signs through the "stars of the air" and the wind, by the discoloring or destruction of all tender and delicate things that the wind harms—such as by stripping away flowers, leaves, boughs, and branches. If the stars of <term>Chaomancy</term> are active <note>original: "moved"</note>, spirits fall from the upper air, and voices and answers are often heard. Trees are also pulled up by the roots, and houses are thrown down. One may see <term>Hobgoblins</term>, household gods, spirits of the air, and <term>forest spirits</term> <note>original: "Woodmen"</note>; furthermore, a heavenly dew and <term>manna</term> <note>a sweet, edible substance traditionally thought to fall from heaven</note> fall upon trees and herbs.

<margin>The signs of Necromancy.</margin>
<term>Necromancy</term> draws forth its signs from the "stars of death," which we call *<term>Evestra</term>* <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, an Evestrum is a prophetic shadow or astral body</note>. These are prophetic spirits that mark the body of a sick or dying person with red, blue, and purple spots; these are certain signs of death within three days of their appearance. They also mark the hands and fingers of people with a clay-like color, which are certain signs of change, whether good or bad. Therefore, when the stars of <term>Necromancy</term> are active, the dead show extraordinary signs, such as bleeding, and voices are heard coming out of the graves. Disturbances and tremors arise in the places where bones are buried, and dead men appear in the form and clothing <note>original: "habit"</note> of living men...

<summary>This page details the various omens and supernatural signs associated with different forms of elemental divination: Pyromancy (fire), Hydromancy (water), Chaomancy (air), and Necromancy (the dead). It explains how elemental spirits and physical disturbances in nature serve as prophetic warnings of change or impending death.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, divination, elemental spirits, Hydromancy, Chaomancy, Necromancy, Evestra, omens, occult philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 25 ---
<meta>This page concludes Book IX of the treatise on "The Nature of Things," continuing the discussion on signs and omens that predict human fate.</meta>

BOOK IX. Of the Nature of Things.
145

They appear to people in visions, in looking-glasses <note>mirrors</note>, in beryls <note>transparent gemstones used as crystals for scrying or divination</note>, in stones, and in waters, taking various forms. *Evestra* <note>original: "Evestra" — In Paracelsian philosophy, these are prophetic spirits or "shadows" of things that are about to happen.</note> (that is, spirits) give their signs by beating, striking, knocking, falling, throwing, and so on. In these cases, a great commotion and noise are heard, but nothing is seen; all of which are certain signs of death, presaging it for the person whose form they take, or for someone in the place where they are heard.

Besides these signatures <note>Signatures: In this context, these are physical signs or omens that reveal a hidden truth or a future event.</note>, many more could be listed. But seeing as they bring with them evil, harmful, and dangerous fantasies, imaginations, and superstitions—which may be a cause of not only some misfortune, but also death—I shall pass over them in silence. We are prohibited from revealing them to others, as they belong to the school of secrets and divine power. Therefore, I shall now bring this book to an end.

...seen in visions, in mirrors, in "hells" <note>original: "Hells"; likely referring to dark cavities, subterranean depths, or perhaps specific dark surfaces used for scrying (divination)</note>, in stones, in waters, and in various shapes. Spirits also give their signs by knocking, striking, falling, casting objects, and so on, where a great commotion <note>original: "hurly burly"</note> and noise is heard, but nothing is seen. All of these are certain signs of death, foretelling <note>original: "presaging"</note> the end for the person in whose house they appear, or for someone in the place where they are heard.

Many more signatures besides these could be listed. However, since they bring with them evil, hurtful, and dangerous fancies, imaginations, and superstitions—which may cause not only misfortune but also death—I shall pass over them in silence. We are prohibited from revealing them, as they belong to the <term>Schools of Secrets</term> <note>Refers to esoteric or occult traditions where hidden knowledge was reserved only for initiates.</note> and to divine power. Therefore, I shall bring this book to an end.

*Philosopher’s Vinegar* <note>original: *Acetum Philosophorum*</note> is a mercurial water; it is otherwise called *Virgin’s milk*, in which they say metals are dissolved.

*Radical Vinegar* <note>original: *Acetum radicale*</note> is vinegar distilled out of its own "root" and "matrix" <note>the source or original substance from which something grows</note>, and is called the Dissolving Water.

*Adamita* is a certain kind of tartar <note>In early chemistry, "tartar" referred to the hard, stony crust found in wine casks; Paracelsus often used the term to describe mineral deposits or "stones" within the human body</note>.

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*<term>Adech</term>* is our interior and invisible man, who represents the forms of all things in our mind, which afterward our outward man frames and imitates with his own hands; both of them work according to their nature.

*<term>Aerdadi</term>* are corporeal spirits living in the air.

*<term>Aes</term>* does not always signify copper <note>Latin: aes</note>, but sometimes gold, silver, or any other metal that is pure and generated by itself, without the mixture of any other metal or stone; from this kind of metal, they formerly made and coined money.

*<term>Aethna</term>* is called subterranean fire, invisible and sulfurous, which burns stones into coals, like jet in the mountains, which are full of resin and bitumen.

*<term>Aestphara</term>* is the burning of flesh, or the substance of the body, into ashes.

*<term>Aethnici</term>* are called fiery spirits, or spiritual beings burning in the fire, which appear in diverse forms and shapes, such as fiery flames, firebrands, or round balls of coals, especially in sulfur mountains.

*<term>Alcali</term>* is the name for all types of salt extracted out of ashes, or the lime <note>original: "calx"</note> of any matter, by boiling it in lye.

*<term>Alcaest</term>*, or *<term>Altaest</term>*, is said to be prepared mercury; some believe it to be tartar, but the author's meaning is more easily understood by the description of its preparation.

*<term>Alchymia</term>* <note>Alchemy</note> is the separation of that which is impure from a purer substance.

*<term>Alcol</term>, <term>Alcool</term>, or <term>Alcohol</term>* is a most subtle powder of any substance.

*<term>Alcool vini</term>* is the spirit of wine <note>original: "Alcool vini"</note> that has been rectified <note>purified through repeated distillation</note>.

<summary>This page provides a glossary of Paracelsian alchemical terms, defining spiritual concepts like the "Adech" alongside physical processes such as the extraction of alkali salts and the rectification of spirits.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, Adech, Aes, Aethna, Alkali, Alcahest, Alcohol, distillation, Paracelsian philosophy, metallurgy</keywords>

## A Chemical Dictionary.

*Alcubrith*, or *Alcur*, or *Alazar* is the same as sulfur.
*Alcosol*, or (as some would have it, *Alcosol*) is *stibium*, or antimony <note>original: "stibium"; the Latin name for antimony, from which the symbol "Sb" is derived</note>.
*Alandahal*, or *Alhandal* is *coloquintida* <note>modern: colocynth, a bitter Mediterranean fruit used as a powerful purgative</note>.
*Alembroth* is salt of mercury, or the "Salt of Philosophers."
*Alembroth purified* is salt of tartar and its magistery <term>a concentrated essence or a precipitate of a substance, believed to hold its highest medicinal power</term>.
*Almizadir* is verdigris <note>a green pigment or crust formed on copper or brass</note>.
*Altey plumbi* is the sweet matter of lead <note>likely "sugar of lead" or lead acetate, which has a deceptively sweet taste but is highly toxic</note>.
*Alusar* is manna.
*Amalgama* <term>amalgam</term> is the process of making gold, silver, or any other metal into a paste by mixing it with quicksilver <term>mercury</term>.
*Amianthus* is a stone similar to *alumen plumosum* <note>literally "feathery alum"; a term often used for fibrous minerals like asbestos</note> in nature and condition; it is not consumed by fire, which is why it is also called the "Salamander" <note>the salamander was a mythical creature believed to be able to live in or endure fire</note>.
*Amidum*, or *Amilum* is the whitest flour made into bread in the sun <note>usually refers to starch</note>.
*Amygdale* <note>literally "almonds"</note> is the name surgeons use for the superfluous flesh <note>the tonsils</note> which grows at the root of the tongue.
*Amnis alcalisatus* is water passing through the chalk of the earth, from which alkali is extracted.
*Anachmus* is an incorporeal spirit.
*Andena* is steel brought from Oriental countries, which melts in the fire like other metals and is cast into several forms.
*Anathron*, or *Anachthron* is a kind of salt growing upon rocks like white and stony moss; some call it saltpeter. The ancients falsely thought it to be the "gall of glass" <note>also known as sandiver; the scum or impurities that float on the surface of molten glass</note>, seeing that it is rather the gall of stones.

# A Chemical Dictionary

*Anatrum* is glass melted into various colors, which they call *smalt* <note>original: "smaltum"</note> or *Saracen earth* <note>original: "terra Sarasenica"</note>.

*Anthos* in plants <note>original: "Vegetables"</note> refers to rosemary flowers, and in metals, it refers to the Elixir or quintessence of gold.

*Anatomia Effata* is the "mother of diseases" <note>In Paracelsian medicine, this refers to the underlying spiritual or structural cause of an illness.</note>.

*Anticar* is Borax.

*Anatris* or *Antaris* is Mercury.

*Aniaday* signifies an eternal spring, a new world to come, or paradise.

*Aniadum* is a celestial body planted in us Christians by the Holy Spirit through the holy sacraments; it is the spiritual man regenerated within us.

*Aniada* are the fruits and virtues of paradise and heaven, as well as the sacraments of Christians. In natural philosophy, it signifies the astral and celestial virtues as they lead to a long life through their influence.

*Aniadus* is the efficacy or inherent power of things.

*Anima* is our Mercury <note>In alchemy, the "soul" (anima) refers to the essential volatile spirit or life-force of a substance.</note>.

*Anodyna* are medicines that induce sleep <note>Modern: Anodynes or sedatives.</note>.

*Anodus* is that which is separated from nourishment by the kidneys.

*Anontagius* is the Philosophers' Stone.

*Anima Saturni* is the sweetness of lead <note>original: "Anima Saturni"; this likely refers to lead acetate, which has a sweet taste and was known as "Sugar of Lead."</note>.

*Annus Platonicus* is a "Platonic Year," meaning a common month or an age.

*Annus Aniadin* is a long life.

*Annora* is the ashes of eggshells or quicklime.

*Anotafier*
*Aliocab* } are Sal Ammoniac <note>original: "Salt Armoniack"; ammonium chloride.</note>

*Anterit* is Mercury.

<header>A Chemical Dictionary.</header>

# A Chemical Dictionary.

<term>Antera</term> is a medicine extracted from hyacinths; it also refers to the yellow substance <note>likely pollen or stamens</note> that grows in the middle of lilies and similar flowers.

<term>Athanor</term> <note>original: Anthonor or Athonar</note> is a type of furnace <note>a self-feeding furnace used by alchemists to maintain a constant heat</note>.

<term>Aphorism</term> <note>original: Aphorismus</note> is a general rule in medicine <note>original: Physick</note> that is accepted as a proven principle.

<term>Heavenly Water</term> <note>original: Aqua Coelestis</note> is rectified wine <note>highly distilled alcohol</note>, so named because its subtlety and purity are thought to resemble the heavens.

<term>Celestial Water</term> <note>original: Aqua celestina</note> is mercurial water.

<term>Corroding Water</term> <note>original: Aqua corrodens</note> is vinegar and all other corrosive liquids.

<term>Water of Wine Dregs</term> <note>original: Aqua fecum vini</note> is a liquid made from the ashes of wine lees <note>sediment</note>, dissolved on a marble slab in the same manner as <term>oil of tartar</term> <note>potassium carbonate solution</note>.

<term>Lubricated Water</term> <note>original: Aqua lubricata</note> is made from mucilaginous (thick or slimy) substances, such as sugar, juleps, and similar preparations.

<term>Permanent Water</term> <note>original: Aqua permanens</note> is a substance made from two "perfect" metallic bodies <note>traditionally gold and silver</note> through a "philosophical solution" <note>an alchemical process of dissolving</note>.

<term>Saturnine Water</term> <note>original: Aqua Saturnia</note> is water that retains the nature of the <term>three principles</term> <note>salt, sulfur, and mercury</note>, such as natural spring waters used for medicinal baths.

<term>Solvent Water</term> <note>original: Aqua solvens</note> is distilled vinegar.

<term>Aquaster</term> is a vision that presents something to the eyes which is not actually there, existing only in appearance <note>a term from Paracelsian philosophy referring to illusions or astral visions</note>.

<term>The Eagle</term> <note>original: Aquila</note> is the queen of birds. In alchemy, the term is used for <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>original: salt Armoniack</note> because of its lightness during sublimation <note>the process of a solid turning directly into vapor</note>. However, the alchemist **Paracelsus** often uses the term to mean **mercury precipitated with gold**.

<term>The Philosophers' Eagle</term> <note>original: Aquila Philosophorum</note> is the "mercury of metals"—that is, metal reduced back into its primary, elemental state.

<meta>This page begins "A Chemical Dictionary," specifically focusing on terms associated with the Paracelsian school of alchemy and natural philosophy.</meta>

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*Arbor Maris* <note>Latin: "Tree of the Sea"</note> is Coral, which grows like a shrub in the sea.

*Arcanum* <note>Latin: "secret"</note> in general signifies anything that is hidden. In the works of *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine</note>, it signifies any secret, incorporeal virtue in natural things, partaking of a perpetual and immortal life derived from heaven. This virtue may also be multiplied by the spagyric <note>original: "Spagiricall"; refers to alchemical processes of separating and recombining the constituents of substances</note> art beyond its former condition.

*Archaltes* is, in *Paracelsus*, the foundation of the earth, or a pillar which seems not to be upheld by its fellows, but as if by a wonderful providence of God.

*Archeius* is the highest, exalted, and invisible spirit which is separated from bodies. It is exalted and ascends; it is the universal hidden nature, operator, and physician in all things. So *Archiatrus* <note>Greek: "chief physician"</note> is the supreme physician of Nature, which hiddenly distributes to every thing and every member their peculiar *Archeius* by means of *Ares*. Also, *Archeius* is the first in Nature—the most secret virtue producing all things out of *Iliastes* <note>original: "Iliaſte"; the primordial matter or chaos from which the universe was formed</note>, being supported by a divine power. 

*Ares* is the dispenser of Nature hidden in all the three principles <note>Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt, known in alchemy as the "Tria Prima"</note>, from which everything has its being. It disposes all things into a particular form, shape, and substance so that they may take on their own proper specific nature and not another's. But you must note the difference between these three in nature, to speak in the manner of the schools: *Iliastes* is the substance of the highest genus (or kind), consisting of the first universal matter of all things, which it first disposes into three kinds: namely, into Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt. The *Archeius* is the first dispenser of Nature, and then it produces all things into their next genera or kinds. Then comes *Ares*...

<summary>This page defines key alchemical terms from the Paracelsian tradition, focusing on the spiritual and material forces—such as the Archeius and the three principles—that govern the formation of the natural world.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, alchemical dictionary, Archeius, Arcanum, Iliastes, Ares, Tria Prima, spagyric art, early modern science</keywords>

# A Chemical Dictionary

<term>Ares</term> another dispenser of Nature, which produces individuals from kinds (or genera), forms, and species.

<term>Ardentia</term> are substances that, having received no fuel, are by their own nature prone to burning, such as amber, turpentine, jet, and similar materials. <note>original: "having received no food" — in this context, "food" refers to external fuel; these are substances the author considers inherently flammable.</note>

<term>Aridura</term> is the total wasting away or consumption of the body and its parts. <note>Aridura was a term used for atrophy or emaciation.</note>

<term>Arles crudum</term> refers to drops falling in the month of June, similar to May dew.

<term>Aromata</term> are all such things which yield a sweet and pleasant smell.

<term>Aroph</term> is mandrake.

<term>Arsaneck</term> is sublimed arsenic.

<term>Arsenicum</term> is the "flashing" of metals, or their salt, or the salt of Saturn <note>original: "Saturne" — in alchemy, Saturn is the symbol for lead.</note>, which in some places is called **Artanek** or **Artanech**.

<term>Artetiscus</term> is a person who lacks any limb or member. <note>original: "wants" — here meaning "to lack."</note>

<term>Arthoicum</term> is a red oil, artificially extracted from the roots of herbs that have been digested with bread in dung. <note>Dung was commonly used in alchemy as a source of gentle, consistent heat for the process of "digestion" (fermentation or slow heating).</note>

<term>Asphaltum</term> is a bitumen extracted from the mud of the earth and water, and is similar to pitch.

<term>Ascendentia signa</term> are called either the stars of the firmament of heaven or the sidereal spirits. <note>original: "sydereall" — relating to the stars or constellations.</note>

<term>Asaphatum</term> is an itch that develops between the skin and the flesh, appearing like worms; if the skin is squeezed, long threads with black heads emerge. <note>This likely describes blackheads (comedones) or a similar skin condition.</note>

<term>Assalais</term> refers to a nutmeg.

<term>Assalia vermes</term> are those worms which breed in wood.

<vocab>Ares, Ardentia, Aridura, Arles crudum, Aromata, Aroph, Arsaneck, Arsenicum, Artetiscus, Arthoicum, Asphaltum, Ascendentia signa, Asaphatum, Assalais, Assalia vermes, chemistry, alchemy, medicine</vocab>

<summary>This page provides a glossary of alchemical, botanical, and medical terms beginning with the letter A, ranging from natural substances like nutmeg and bitumen to complex Paracelsian concepts like "Ares."</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Paracelsus, arsenic, Saturn, medicinal oils, skin conditions, astrological signs, chemical terminology</keywords>

--- Page 36 ---
<meta>This page continues definitions from the previous page, beginning with the conclusion of an entry describing wood-boring insects.</meta>

<header>*A Chemical Dictionary.*</header>

wood, or between two boards, and they are called *Teredones.* <note>original: "Te- redones"; wood-boring worms or shipworms.</note>

*Asthma* is a disease of the lungs causing difficulty of breathing.

*Astrum* <note>Latin: "star"</note> is in this context called the <term>virtue</term> <note>original: "vertue"; meaning the inherent power or medicinal efficacy of a substance</note> and power gained by the preparation of things. For example, the "star" of sulfur is its inflammability, which is turned into a most excellent oil. Similarly, the "star" of salt is its dissolution into water or oil, which thereby receives more virtues than it had before. The "star" of Mercury is its sublimation, by which it acquires a wonderful power and virtue that is greater and more refined than its natural state.

*Astrum ex igne* <note>Latin: "star from fire"</note> is a burning fire of great intensity.

*Athanor*, or *Athanar*, is a furnace used in the <term>spagyric art</term> <note>original: "spagirical Art"; alchemy, specifically the process of separating and recombining natural elements</note>, but especially a <term>reverberatory</term> <note>a furnace that reflects or "reverberates" heat onto a substance</note> furnace, though it is sometimes used for other purposes as the craftsman sees fit.

*Atramentum* <note>Latin: "blacking" or "ink"</note> signifies various things depending on the adjective added to it. For instance, the kind that shoemakers use is copperas <note>original: "Copperis"; iron(II) sulfate, used as a dye or pigment</note>, specifically the kind that is red inside. The kind that writers use is called ink or soot, and it is called blacking; the term also refers to all kinds of copperas.

*Attractiva* are called magnetic medicines, which have the power to draw to themselves such things as are composed of similar attractive qualities.

*Augurista* is the name for a superstitious practitioner <note>original: "Artist"</note> who practices his art using mirrors, crystals, and water, and especially by the singing and flight of birds, so that he may predict the future through them; he also observes other rituals for the same purpose.

*Aurum planatum* <note>Latin: "flattened gold"</note> is what they call gold leaf or hammered gold.

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*Potable Gold* <note>original: Aurum potabile</note> is a liquid form of gold made without any corrosive substance. Very few people know how to make it, including those who prepare it daily to the destruction, rather than the health, of men.

*The Gold of Life* <note>original: Aurum vitæ</note> is precipitated gold that has been heated <term>reverberated</term> to the highest degree of redness, resembling ground cinnabar <term>mercury sulfide</term>. The best variety is that which is made and precipitated with its own proper Mercury.

*The Bird of Hermes* <note>original: Avis Hermetis</note> is the "Mercury of the Philosophers," which ascends and then descends for nourishment <note>This refers to the process of distillation and condensation within an alchemical vessel</note>.

*Living Gold* <note>original: Aurum vivum</note> is sometimes used as a name for quicksilver <term>mercury</term>.

*Austromancy* <note>original: Austromantia</note> is a certain kind of superstition invented concerning the observation of winds. For instance, when the "stars of the winds" break forth with great vehemence contrary to their usual custom, men who are more idle than rational pronounce a prophecy of something to come.

*Fat of Mummy* <note>original: Axungia de Mumia</note>, or *Mummy of Marrow* <note>original: Mumia de Medullis</note>, is the marrow of bones.

*Azemasor* is minium <term>red lead</term> or cinnabar.

*Azoth* is quicksilver extracted out of any physical body; it is properly called the "mercury of the body." However, in the works of Paracelsus, it refers to the universal medicine of all things.

<summary>This page defines several alchemical terms starting with 'A,' focusing on various forms of gold, mercury, and specialized terminology like Azoth and Austromancy.</summary>
<keywords>Aurum potabile, Aurum vitæ, Avis Hermetis, Aurum vivum, Austromantia, Axungia de Mumia, Mumia de Medullis, Azemasor, Azoth, Paracelsus, alchemy, chemistry, cinnabar, mercury</keywords>

# B.

---

**Balneum Mariæ**, or *Maris* <note>original: "Maris," meaning "of the sea"</note>, as many call it, is a furnace for distillation containing water. In this warm water, chemical <term>vessels</term> are placed for the <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "putrefaction," the process of rotting or breaking down matter to release its components</note> of the substances they contain, as well as for their separation and for performing operations involving moist evaporation <note>original: "ascensions"</note>.

*Balneum roris* <note>original: "Bath of dew"</note> is a furnace in which the distillation vessels are placed only over the steam of the water, so that the liquid water does not touch the vessel itself; it is also called a vapor furnace.

*Balsamum* <note>Balsam</note> is a substance within bodies that preserves things from rotting. It is both internal and external. Internal balsam in humans is a certain balanced <note>original: "temperate"</note> substance—neither bitter, sweet, nor sour, and not a mineral salt, but the "salt of liquid" which strongly protects the body from decay. External balsam is turpentine that has never been exposed to fire but has been naturally processed <note>original: "digested"</note>.

*Balsamum de Mumiis* <note>Balsam of Mummies</note> is that which is extracted from flesh.

*Balsamum Elementorum externum* <note>External Balsam of the Elements</note> is a liquid of external <term>Mercury</term> <note>In alchemy, Mercury is one of the three "Primes" or fundamental principles of matter</note>—that is, the <term>Mummie</term> <note>In this context, "mummy" refers to the vital life-force or preservative essence within a substance</note> of external elements, one of the three principles, and the celestial essence of things.

*Baul* is urine.

<vocab>Balneum Mariae, Balneum roris, Balsamum, Balsamum de Mumiis, Balsamum Elementorum externum, Baul, Chymicall, distillation, putrefaction, Mercury, Mummie, Turpentine, firmamentall essence</vocab>

<summary>This page defines various types of alchemical baths used for heating substances and explains the concept of "Balsam" as a preservative life-force found both within the human body and in nature.</summary>
<keywords>Balneum Mariae, bain-marie, distillation, balsam, alchemy, mercury, mummy, turpentine, chemistry, 17th century science</keywords>

-># A Chemical Dictionary.<-

*Baurac* <note>original: "Baurac"; often refers to borax or other alkaline salts.</note> is any kind of brine.

*Berillus* is a crystalline looking glass <note>original: "looking-glasse"</note> superstitiously consecrated by augurs <note>Ancient Roman religious officials who interpreted omens and practiced divination.</note>.

*Berillistica* is the art of observing visions in those kinds of glasses.

*Bismutum* is the lightest, whitest, and basest kind of lead <note>Now known as the element Bismuth.</note>.

*Bitumen* is a certain kind of slimy mud extracted from water; it is similar to pitch and is, in a sense, the pitch of the earth <note>A naturally occurring petroleum product used as an adhesive or sealant.</note>.

*Botin* is turpentine.

*Bothor* refers to wheals <note>Raised, itchy red bumps on the skin.</note> or pustules <note>original: "pustles"; small blisters or pimples containing pus.</note>.

*Brassadella*, or *Brassatella*, is adder’s-tongue <note>A type of fern (Ophioglossum) historically used in herbal medicine.</note>.

*Brunus* is St. Anthony's fire <note>A historical name for various skin conditions, most commonly ergotism or erysipelas (a bacterial infection).</note>.

*Bruta* is an influence of a celestial virtue <note>The idea that heavenly or divine power influences the natural world.</note> which is revealed to humans by animals <note>original: "Brutes"</note>. Examples include the use of celandine <note>original: "Sallendine"; a plant long believed to be used by swallows to heal their young’s eyes.</note> by the swallow, or the use of salt in a clyster <note>original: "Glyster"; an enema. Ancient lore claimed storks taught humans this practice.</note> by the stork, and many more of that kind.

*Butyrum Saturni* <note>Latin: "Butter of Saturn."</note> is that which was referred to above as *Altey*; it is the "sweetness of lead" <note>This refers to lead acetate, which has a sweet taste but is highly toxic.</note>.

# C.

**Kabbalah** <note>original: "Cabela" or "Cabalia"</note> is a most secret science which is said to have been delivered by divine inspiration along with the Law of Moses, according to the assertions of the Hebrew Rabbis. The Persians were very diligent seekers of this art and were also professors of it, as is shown by what they called their wise men. They call their priests "wise men" and consider them the most skillful in all secrets—such as the three [Magi] who came from the East to worship Christ, and who were not kings, as the ignorant common people <note>original: "vulgar"</note> believe them to be. 

This science was not originally recorded in writing but was passed down by word of mouth. However, after a time, superstitious men—acting like apes <note>The author uses "apes" to describe those who mindlessly mimic or distort sacred traditions without understanding them.</note>—began to write it down, so that it has eventually degenerated into monstrous superstition. Because of this, <term>magic</term>—which is the true wisdom received through divine inspiration—is in these times categorized as <term>necromancy</term> and <term>black magic</term> <note>original: "Nigromancy"</note>. Consequently, it is now seen as an offense to be a wise man, and anyone who practices it openly puts himself in danger. Meanwhile, to publicly teach the foolishness of infidels is considered commendable and a gift of singular wisdom; today, no man is judged wise unless he is the greatest fool.

**Caballi** <note>original: "Caballi" or "Cabales"</note> are ghosts or the "astral stars" of people who died before their natural time.

<summary>This page defines Kabbalah as a divine secret science corrupted over time into superstition and discusses how true magic has been unfairly maligned as necromancy. It also defines "Caballi" as the astral spirits of those who suffer premature deaths.</summary>

<keywords>Kabbalah, Magi, magic, necromancy, Caballi, astral stars, Paracelsian terminology, superstition</keywords>

# A Chemical Dictionary

*Calcedonium tartarum* is a harmful substance in the human body, which arises due to a failure of the expulsive faculty <note>the body's natural ability to eliminate waste</note> in not expelling what has been separated.

*Cafa* is camphor.

*Calcanthum* is vitriol <note>a group of sulfate minerals often used in alchemical experiments</note>.

*Calcinatum majus* is the name for everything made sweet by the spagyric art <note>original: Spagiricall; the alchemical practice of separating and recombining the components of a substance to purify it</note> that was not sweet by its own nature—such as the sweetness of mercury, lead, salt, or the like. This is also called their "soul," and it quickly heals <note>original: consolidate</note> any wound.

*Calcinatum minus* is everything that is naturally sweet and very healing, such as sugar, manna, wild honey, nostoc <term>a jelly-like cyanobacteria once believed to be "star-jelly" fallen from the sky with medicinal properties</term>, and the like.

*Callena* is a kind of saltpeter.

*Caleruth* is a sign of a desire for the first *perpetuum* <note>an eternal or original state of being</note>, as when any thing desires to return to the "first matter" from which it originated.

*Calliette* are yellow mushrooms found on juniper trees.

*Calx Mercurii* is precipitated mercury.

*Calx Veneris* is verdigris <note>the green pigment or crust that forms on copper</note>.

*Calx Saturni* is minium <note>red lead</note>.

*Calx Iovis* is spirit of tin.

*Calx Martis* is Crocus of Steel or Iron <note>a reddish-brown iron oxide used as a pigment or polishing agent</note>.

*Calx Solis* is calcined gold.

*Calx Luna* is the azure flower of silver.

*Calx permanens* or *fixa*, is an incombustible matter.

*Calx peregrinorum* is tartar.

*Calx lignorum* is wood ashes.

*Calcitis* is a stone from which brass is extracted <note>original: boiled</note>.

*Chaomancy* is an art of foretelling the future by observing the air.

<summary>This page provides a glossary of alchemical terms beginning with 'C,' focusing on medicinal substances, various "calxes" (metallic oxides), and divinatory practices like chaomancy.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Calcinatum, spagyric art, calx, metallurgy, medicinal chemistry, chaomancy, Paracelsian terms</keywords>

--- Page 42 ---
<meta>This page continues a glossary of alchemical and Paracelsian terms, following the previous section on terms beginning with "C." The catchword at the bottom, "Cer-," indicates that the next page will begin with terms starting with those letters.</meta>

<header>A Chemical Dictionary.</header>

*Chaos*, besides the confused and unshaped matter of all things, is understood by *Paracelsus* to mean the air; it is also understood as *Iliaste* or *Iliastro* <note>original: "Illiaste, or Illiastro"; Paracelsian terms for the primeval chaos or the spiritual force from which elements are formed</note>.

*Claretta* is the white of eggs.

*Cambuca* is an abscess <note>original: "aposteme"</note> or ulcer in the groin.

*Caput corvi* <note>Latin: "Raven's head"</note> is antimony.

<margin>y</margin> *Caput mortuum* <note>Latin: "Dead head"</note> is the residue <note>original: "feces"</note> remaining after distillation and sublimation.

*Carbones caeli* <note>Latin: "Coals of heaven"</note> are the stars.

*Carbunculus* <note>Latin: "Carbuncle"</note> is an abscess or a plague-related ulcer.

*Cardonium* is a medicinal wine made of herbs.

*Carena* is the twentieth part of a drop.

*Cassatum* is weak and dead blood in the veins that obstructs the movement of healthy blood.

*Caseus praeparatus* <note>Latin: "Prepared cheese"</note> is the viscous residue remaining at the bottom of the milk that runs out of cheese.

*Cathimia* is the scum <note>original: "spume"</note> of silver.

*Cauda vulpis rubicundi* <note>Latin: "Tail of the red fox"</note> is red lead <note>original: "Minium"</note>.

*Cautela* <note>Latin: "Caution" or "Security"</note> in the spagyric <note>alchemical</note> art is a certain kind of skill gained through practice, by which practitioners of the art more easily perform their work and perfect their processes.

*Cauterium* <note>Latin: "Cautery"</note> is a surgical <note>original: "chirurgicall"</note> instrument used to open the skin by burning; it is also a medicine that achieves the same effect, but without pain.

*Cedurini* are slow or dull-witted people.

*Cenigdam*, or *Ceningotam*, is a surgical instrument used to open the skull <note>original: "Cranium"</note> during epileptic seizures.

*Ceniotemium* is mercury prepared to treat venereal disease <note>likely syphilis</note>.

*Cinificatum* is the same as *calcinated* <note>original: "Calcinatum"</note>, meaning burnt to ashes.

It defines various substances like antimony and red lead using metaphorical Latin names and describes early surgical tools and medical conditions.</summary>

# A Chemical Dictionary

**Cervicula** is the spirit made from the bone found in the heart of a stag <note>original: "Hart"</note>.

**Character veneris** is love, which acts as a shield to keep off danger.

**Cherva** is *Cataputia*, that is, the herb Spurge.

**Cheiri** in the works of Paracelsus: if it is used alone without any addition and refers to minerals, it signifies quicksilver <note>mercury</note>. If it refers to plants, it signifies vegetable flowers. However, when it is found with an addition in this manner—*flos Cheiri* <note>flower of Cheiri</note>—it signifies the white Elixir made of silver, just as *Flos Anthos* <note>flower of Anthos</note> signifies the red Elixir.

**Cherio** is the hidden <note>original: "occult"</note> accidental virtue of the external elements, rather than the qualities of heat, cold, and the like.

**Cherionium** is a substance in which nature cannot be altered, such as crystal, which is so hardened by nature that it cannot be melted like those things made by human art <note>In this context, "art" refers to laboratory processes or technology</note>.

**Cherubin** is a celestial virtue, influence, dominion, and power above all other dominions and powers, proceeding from God and descending upon the earth and all people. Paracelsus speaks extensively of this divine glory in his explanations of the Psalms of David.

**Chifir Minerale** is interpreted by some as gold, but I judge it, based on what has been written previously, to be the sulfur of any metal.

**Chiromancy**, according to Paracelsus, does not only treat the lines of the hands, but also the whole body—and not only of humans, but of all other natural things as well <note>This suggests a belief that all of nature contains "signatures" or signs that can be read</note>.

**Chymia** is the art of separating the pure from the impure and of creating essences <note>This is a foundational definition of alchemy and early chemistry</note>.

**Chybur**, or **Cibur**, is sulfur.

<summary>This page defines various alchemical, botanical, and spiritual terms, specifically focusing on the specialized terminology used by Paracelsus regarding elixirs, minerals, and the purification of substances.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, Chymia, alchemy, sulfur, quicksilver, Chiromancy, Spurge, Elixir, Cherubin</keywords>
