-># A Chemical Dictionary.<-

*Chrysocolla* <note>original: Chrysocolla; often refers to copper minerals or malachite</note> is a kind of green earth like **verdigris**.

*Chrysos* <note>original Greek: chrysos</note> is **gold**.

*Chymus* is **dregs** <note>original: Feces; in this context, it refers to the sediment or residue left after a process</note>.

*Cineritium* <note>original: Cineritium; from the Latin for 'ash'</note> is a **cement** of gold or silver, which some call *Regale* <note>original: Regale; a 'royal' process used to purify gold</note>.

*Citrinulus* is **pale crystal**.

*Citrinulum* is a **transparent salt** made out of calcined **vitriol** <note>original: vitriall; vitriol refers to metallic sulfates</note>.

*Citrinula* is a blistering **herb** called **crowfoot** <note>likely Ranunculus, known for causing skin irritation</note>.

*Cist*, or *Kist*, is the quantity of **two gallons of wine**.

*Clissus* <note>a term used by Paracelsus</note> is the **hidden power** <note>original: occult vertue</note> of things returning from where they came, such as the power of an herb returning into the root in autumn.

*Coagulatio* <note>Coagulation</note> is the **thickening of a liquid substance** <note>original: making of a thin thing thick</note>.

*The Spagyric Heaven* <note>original: Cælum Spagiricum</note> is the **upper part** of a chemical vessel.

*The Heaven of the Philosophers* <note>original: Cælum Philosophorum</note> is any **quintessence** or **universal medicine**, especially the **Philosopher's Stone**.

*The Heavens of the planets* <note>original: Cæli planetarum</note> are their **proper orbits and their spheres**.

*Cementum* <note>Cement</note> is a **dry corrosion** caused when any metallic body is calcined <note>term: calcined; reduced to powder by heat</note> with salts or similar drying substances.

*Cohobatio* <note>Cohobation</note> is the **repeated distilling** of a liquid from its base substance, by frequently pouring the liquid back onto it.

*Cohoph*, or *Cohop*, is the same as **cohobation**.

*Cohos* is whatever the **skin contains** in the whole body.

*Colcothar* is **calcined vitriol**, or the *worthless residue* <note>original: caput Mortuum; literally 'dead head'—the dregs remaining after distillation</note> of oil of vitriol.

# A Chemical Dictionary

<sig>Ccc</sig>

*Collatenna* is an herb called Lion’s-foot, or a certain styptic <term>styptic: a substance used to stop bleeding by contracting tissues</term> medicine.

*Colliquation* is melting, such as when metals are melted.

*Colica* is tartar <note>original: "Tartar"; in Paracelsian medicine, this refers to stony deposits or metabolic waste formed in the body</note> dissolved in the bowels, or a fixed disease in the colon.

*Coleritium* is a liquor compounded of the corrosive materials of metals.

*Cometz* is half a drop.

*Complexio* is the nature of any part of the body, or the balance of heat, cold, and similar qualities <note>referring to the "complexion" or temperament of the body's humors</note>.

*Compositum* <note>Latin: a compound</note>, used on its own, refers to a body or substance that has not been separated into its components.

*Confirmamentum* is the body of a star within a human being, or an astral body <note>the "astral body" was believed to be a celestial component of the human soul that connects it to the stars</note>.

*Confortativa*, or *Confortantia*, are medicines which comfort the heart and strengthen Nature <note>here meaning the body's inherent vital force</note>.

*Congelativa* are medicines which stop any flux <note>an abnormal discharge of fluids from the body</note>.

*Congluten* is that which is turned into a viscous <note>thick or sticky</note> matter through putrefaction.

*Consolidativa* are external medicines which surgeons <note>original: "Chirurgions"</note> use for healing and drying wounds and ulcers.

*Constellatio* is the impression of the superior stars, or the influence of their powers <note>original: "vertues"</note> upon inferior bodies on Earth.

*Constrictiva* are styptic <term>styptic: a substance used to stop bleeding by contracting tissues</term> medicaments which surgeons <note>original: "Chirurgions"</note> use.

*Contorsio* is a torment or twisting of the bowels.

*Contractio* is a weakness of the limbs <note>original: "members"</note> and the involuntary drawing of them together.

*Conservativa* are medicaments which preserve Nature from decay <note>original: "putrefaction"</note>, being substances that are full of astral power <note>original: "vertue"</note>.

*Costrum* is the middle part of the diaphragm.

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*Cor* <note>Latin: heart</note> among metals is called gold.
*Corbatum* is copper.
*Cornu cervi* <note>Latin: hart's horn</note> is, among the chemists, the nose of a still; according to Paracelsus <note>a famous 16th-century Swiss physician and alchemist</note>, it is a vulnerary herb <note>vulnerary: a plant used to heal wounds</note>.
*Corpus* <note>Latin: body</note> is the subject in which the virtues <note>virtues: the inherent powers or medicinal properties</note> of things lie hidden.
*Celestial bodies of the Spagyrists* <note>original: *Corpora cœlestia Spagyrorum*; Spagyrists were alchemists who practiced the "spagyric art" of separating and recombining natural substances for medicine</note> are astral virtues <note>astral virtues: powers believed to be derived from the stars or heavenly bodies</note> within their matter.
*Super-celestial bodies* <note>original: *Corpora supercælestia*</note> are those bodies that are not known by the senses, but by reason.
*Invisible body* <note>original: *Corpus invisibile*</note> is the soul, which is corporeal <note>corporeal: having a physical nature or substance</note> in terms of its sustenance, and invisible because of its spirituality.
*Corrosives* <note>original: *Corrosiva*</note> are medicines that surgeons <note>original: "Chirurgions"</note> use to eat away excess <note>original: "superfluous"</note> flesh.
*Bark of the Sea* <note>original: *Cortex Maris*</note> is the "Vinegar of Philosophers" <note>a metaphorical term for a powerful chemical solvent used in alchemy</note>.
*Coruscus* is mouse-ear <note>a type of flowering plant</note>.
*Cotoronium* is a liquid.
*Blood of salt* <note>original: *Cruor salis*</note> is salt separated from the primary salt due to a failure <note>original: "fault"</note> in the second digestion <note>digestion: in alchemy, the process of refining or maturing a substance through gentle, continuous heat</note>.
*Cucurbita* <note>Latin: gourd</note> is a vessel shaped like a gourd <note>used as the lower part of a still</note>.
*Cycima* is litharge <note>lead monoxide, a byproduct of refining silver</note>.
*Cydar* is Jupiter <note>in alchemical symbolism, Jupiter represents the metal tin</note>.
*Cyphantum* is a distillation vessel, or the art of distillation itself.

# D.

*Dardo* is a successive generation by propagation.

*Daura* is hellebore <note>original: Hellebor</note>.

*Demotinus lapsus* is sudden death.

*Descensorium* is a chemical <note>original: Chymicall</note> furnace in which the liquid falls downward from the gross matter.

*Derfes* is a hidden <note>original: occult</note> vapor of the earth, by reason of which all kinds of wood grow and increase.

*Deliquium* is a cold descension, when solid <note>original: coagulated</note> bodies are dissolved into a liquid in any cold place.

*Diaceltateon* is a special remedy against fevers.

*Diameter Spagiricus* is a temperament <note>original: Diameter Spagiricus; likely referring to the alchemical balance or "spagyric" proportions of elements</note>.

*Diapensia* is *Alchemilla* <note>original: Alchamilla</note>, that is, Lady's mantle.

*Diaphanum* is that which is transparent.

*Diaphoreticum* is a sweating medicine <note>a diaphoretic</note>.

*Diasatyrion* is a medicinal preparation <note>original: confection</note> used to provoke lust <note>an aphrodisiac</note>.

*Diatessadelton* is precipitated mercury <note>original: Mercury</note>.

*Dienez* are spirits that dwell among hard stones.

*Digestio* <note>Digestion</note> is a chemical operation, similar to the digestion of a man's stomach, in which and by which the matter is refined <note>original: decocted</note> to separate the pure from the impure.

*Discus solis* is quicksilver <note>mercury</note> made out of gold.

*Divinatio* is prophesying or foretelling.

*Divertaluim*, or *Divertellum*, is a generation made by the elements.

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*Dracunculus* is *Braffatella*, that is, Adder's tongue <note>Adder's tongue is a type of fern, *Ophioglossum vulgatum*, historically used in herbal medicine.</note>.
*Dubelech* is the cavity of an abscess <note>original: "imposteme," a swelling containing pus</note>.
*Duelech* is a kind of tartar in the human body, such as a stone in the bladder or any other part that is spongy and dangerous.
*Duenech* is Antimony <note>A brittle, silvery-gray metalloid often used in alchemical preparations.</note>.
*Dulcedo Saturni* <note>Latin: "Sweetness of Saturn"</note> is *Altey* or ceruse <note>White lead, a pigment often used in alchemical processes.</note>.
*Durdales* are physical spirits inhabiting trees.
*Diota* is a circulating vessel <note>A type of alchemical flask with two handles used for circulation and distillation.</note>.

---

# E.

---

*Delphus* is anyone who prophesies <note>original: "prognosticates"</note> by the nature of the elements.
*Edir* is steel or iron.
*Electrum* is sometimes taken for amber, but in Paracelsus's works, it is a mixture of a compound metal made by melting all the seven planets <note>The "seven planets" refer to the seven metals associated with celestial bodies: gold (Sun), silver (Moon), mercury (Mercury), copper (Venus), iron (Mars), tin (Jupiter), and lead (Saturn).</note> into one body.
*Elementum* <note>Element</note> in Paracelsus's view is the corruptible and transient essence of the world and of all things that are subject to change.
*Elephas* is *Aqua fortis* <note>Latin: "strong water"; nitric acid, used to dissolve metals.</note>.
*Elixir* is properly a ferment, the smallest part of which turns the entire mass of any substance into its own kind; it is also the essence of any thing.
*Elevation* is the process of making a substance subtle <note>In alchemy, this refers to refining, vaporizing, or spiritualizing a material.</note>.
*Emunctorium* <note>Emunctory</note> is the place of expulsion for any waste or excrement.

->### A Chemical Dictionary<-

*Enochdianum* is that which has a long life.
*Entali* is flaked alum <note>original: Allum</note>, and sometimes it is made spagyrically <term>alchemically prepared</term> from salt gem <note>original: salt gemme; a type of rock salt</note>.
*Enur* is a hidden <note>original: occult</note> vapor of the water, from which stones are formed <note>original: bred</note>.
*Erodinium* is a sign of anything that is to come.
*Escara*, or *Estphara*, is black and dead flesh caused by a caustic substance; it is commonly called an eschar <term>a dry slough or scab caused by a burn or cauterization</term>.
*Essara* are wheals <term>small, itchy swellings on the skin, such as hives</term>.
*Essatum essentiale* is the essential power and virtue that exists in vegetables and minerals.
*Essatum vinum* is rectified spirit of wine <term>highly concentrated alcohol</term>.
*Essentia quinta* <note>Latin: "fifth essence" or quintessence</note>, according to Paracelsus's definition, is a specific matter extracted from things and purified of all impurity and corruption, which makes it incorruptible.
*Essila* is a discoloration <note>original: tincture</note> produced by the heat of the sun on the face or any other part of the body <note>likely referring to freckles or tanning</note>.
*Essodinum* is a certain omen <note>original: presage</note> of future events based on their outward marks.
*Esthionemus* is a very severe abscess <note>original: corrupt imposteme</note> that consumes an entire limb, like a "wolf" <note>a historical term for a devouring ulcer or lupus</note>.
*Evestrum* is a prophetic spirit which foretells the future through signs or preceding omens.
*Excrementa* are all superfluous materials expelled by nature.
*Exitura* is any abscess <note>original: aposteme</note> that discharges pus or matter.
*Exaltatio* <note>Exaltation</note> is the process of refining or purifying a substance to make it more "subtle" or potent.
*Exorcista* <note>Exorcist</note> is a superstitious practitioner <note>original: artist</note> who summons spirits.
*Extractum* <note>Extract</note> is that which is drawn out from a physical mass <note>original: corporeall concretion</note>, leaving the coarse material <note>original: grossenesse</note> behind.
*Ezezick* is salt.

# F.

***Faba*** <note>Latin: bean</note> is the third part of a scruple <term>scruple: a small unit of weight used by apothecaries, equal to 20 grains or 1/24th of an ounce</term>.

***Fabiola*** is the flower of beans.

***Fel Draconis*** <note>Latin: Dragon’s gall</note> is mercury <note>original: "Quickſilver"</note> derived from tin.

***Fel vitri*** <note>Latin: Gall of glass</note> is the scum or froth <note>original: "ſpume"</note> of glass.

***Fermentum*** <note>Latin: Ferment</note> is a fixed substance <note>original: "fixt matter"</note> which transforms another substance into its own nature and stability <note>original: "fixedneſſe"</note>.

***Ficus cutis*** <note>Latin: Skin-fig</note> is a wart growing on the skin of horses' legs; it is also used as a medicine to treat diseases of the womb <note>original: "matrix"</note>.

***Fido*** is a name for mercury <note>original: "Quickſilver"</note>, and sometimes gold.

***Fida*** is silver, and sometimes gold.

***Filius unius Diei*** <note>Latin: Son of one day</note> is a name for the Philosopher’s Stone.

***Filum arsenicale*** <note>Latin: Arsenical thread</note> is a name for the Philosopher’s Stone.

***Fimus equinus*** <note>Latin: Horse dung</note> refers to a method of digestion <term>digestion: a process of slow, gentle heating used to soften or decompose substances</term> performed in any way, whether by using horse manure, warm ashes, or water.

***Filtratio*** <note>Latin: Filtration</note> is the act of straining a substance through a woolen cloth, paper, etc.

***Flagae*** are spirits who possess knowledge of the hidden <note>original: "occult"</note> secrets of men.

***Flos cheiri*** <note>Latin: Flower of Cheiri</note> is the essence of gold.

***Flos sectarum Croe*** <note>Latin: Flower of the sectors of Crocus</note> is mace <note>original: "flower of Nutmeg"</note>, or as some believe, saffron.

***Faedula*** is a type of mushroom.

***Fixatio*** <note>Latin: Fixation</note> is the process of making a volatile substance—one that normally "flies" or evaporates in heat—able to endure the fire.

<summary>This page provides a glossary of alchemical terms starting with the letter 'F,' defining various substances, mystical names for the Philosopher's Stone, and chemical processes like filtration and fixation.</summary>

->A Chemical Dictionary.<-

*Folia aurea* <note>original: "Folia daure"; literally "golden leaves"</note> are leaves of gold.

*Fons Philosophorum* <note>The Philosophers' Fountain</note> is a *Balneum Mariae* <note>original: "Balneum Maris"; literally "Mary's Bath," a double boiler used in alchemy to provide gentle, even heat</note>.

*Formæ rerum* <note>The forms of things</note> are called the influences of celestial bodies, which inferior <note>lower or earthly</note> bodies receive from them; or, they are the virtues <note>inherent powers or qualities</note> of any thing.

*Formicæ* <note>literally "ants"</note> are small abscesses <note>original: "impostemes"</note> similar to warts.

*Fugile* is an abscess <note>original: "aposteme"</note> in the ears.

*Fuligo Metallorum* <note>Soot of Metals</note> is sometimes called arsenic, and sometimes it is used to refer to mercury.

*Fulmen* <note>literally "lightning"</note> is purified silver.

*Fumigatio* <note>Fumigation</note> is the process of reducing a substance to powder <note>original: "calcining"</note> using a sharp, corroding vapor.

*Fusio* <note>Fusion</note> is melting, or making any substance flow in the fire.

*Gamonynum* is that one and only anatomy of all things.

***Gelion*** is a leaf.

***Geomancy*** is the most well-known art of the earth, but in this context, it refers to the "stars of the earth" manifesting themselves to men, so that they may use them as a basis for prophecy.

***Geluta*** is the herb called Chameleon <note>original: *Chameleon*; likely refers to the plant *Carlina acaulis*, or "stemless carline thistle"</note>.

***Gemma tartarea*** <note>Latin: "tartar gem"</note> is the name of the stone produced from transparent and clear tartar <term>tartar</term> <note>a potassium salt deposit found in wine casks, frequently used in alchemical preparations</note>.

***Genula*** is a plant that results from the degeneration of a parsnip through transplanting.

***Gibar*** is a metallic medicine.

***Gigantes*** <note>Giants</note> are men who exceed the natural bounds of human size.

***Gluten*** is any sticky or viscous substance in the body, from which many stubborn diseases arise.

***Glutinis tenacitas*** <note>Latin: "tenacity of glue"</note> is mineral resin.

***Gluta*** is the power or effectiveness of sticky pitch.

***Gnomi*** <note>Gnomes</note> are called *homunculi* <note>original: *homunciones*; small, artificial, or spirit-beings</note>, or rather physical spirits living underground; they are otherwise described as Pygmies who are only a cubit <note>about 18 inches</note> tall.

***Græca Magia*** <note>Greek Magic</note> is a superstitious art invented by the Greeks, by which they made things appear that did not actually exist.

***Gravus*** is the stone porphyry <note>original: *Porphyrites*</note>; it is used as a marble slab to dissolve substances in the cold.

***Grilla***, or ***Grillus***, is vitriol <term>vitriol</term> <note>a sulfate of various metals, usually iron or copper</note> that has naturally dissolved into water.

***Guarini*** are men who live by the influence of the heavens.

***Gama*** is quicksilver <note>mercury</note>.

***Gamicula*** is Valerian <note>a flowering plant used medicinally</note>.

*Gutta rosacea* <note>A skin condition now known as rosacea.</note> is a redness, especially in the face, similar to the coloration seen at the beginning of leprosy.

# H.

---

*Hadid* <note>Arabic: hadīd</note> is iron.

*Halis* <note>Greek: hals</note> is salt.

*Harmel* is the seed of wild rue.

*Haro* is a kind of fern.

*Hel* is honey.

*Henricus rubeus* <note>Latin: "Red Henry"</note> is the colcothar <note>original: "Colcathar"; the red oxide of iron remaining after the distillation of sulfuric acid.</note> of vitriol.

*Hellebria* is a kind of black hellebore that bears red flowers.

*Heliotropium* is the balm of Paracelsus.

*Helismidan* is a balsamic mummy <note>In alchemy, "mummy" refers to a vital essence or medicinal preparation derived from organic matter.</note>.

*Hinicula*, *genicula*, or *gumicula* is valerian.

*Homunculus*, according to Paracelsus, is a man created artificially; the process for making one is described in detail on the eighth page of the preceding treatise. Sometimes the term is also used to refer to superstitious images.

*Horion* is the mercury of gold.

*Humor vitae* <note>Latin: "humor of life"</note> is the radical moisture <note>The essential, life-sustaining fluid believed by early physicians to be inherent in all living beings.</note>.

*Hydri* are juices or fruits.

*Hydromancy* is an art derived from the "stars of the water" when they reveal themselves to humanity through signs such as unusual floods and similar occurrences.

*Hydropiper* is water pepper <note>original: "Arsmart"</note> marked with red specks or spots.

# I

*Iaffa*: Is the herb of the Trinity <note>original: "hearb of the Trinity"; likely referring to the wild pansy (*Viola tricolor*), which was associated with the Trinity because of its three-colored petals.</note>.

*Icteritia rubea*: Is an *erysipelas* <note>original: *Erisipelas*; a bacterial skin infection that causes a painful, bright red rash.</note>.

*Idea*: Is a figure of a thing conceived in the imagination as a perpetual being, or a pattern for all things of that nature.

*Idiotæ*: Are those who despise true arts, even though they may be skillful practitioners of false ones. <note>original: *Idiotæ*; here the author uses the term to describe people who lack specialized alchemical knowledge or who follow traditional, "false" medicine.</note>

*Iefahach*: Is supernatural.

*Ignis leonis*: Is the element of fire itself <note>original: *Ignis leonis*, literally "the fire of the lion."</note>.

*Ignis Persicus*: Is a hot, burning ulcer <note>original: *Ignis Persicus*, or "Persian fire," a historical term for conditions such as shingles or anthrax.</note>.

*Ignis pruinus adeptus*: Is the quintessence of vitriol <term>vitriol: a metal sulfate or sulfuric acid</term> purified with tartar <term>tartar: a salt deposit found in wine barrels, used in various alchemical processes</term>.

*Ilech primum*: Is the first principle.

*Ilech supernaturale*: Is a conjunction of the supernatural and firmamental stars with the inferior stars of terrestrial things and with wine.

*Ilech magnum*: Is an "Ascendant" or a star of a medicine which is taken along with the remedy and in which the medicine is hidden; just as the superior stars are in the heavens, so also are the inferior stars within man.

*Ilech crudum*: Is a composition of the "first matter" of the three first principles <note>In the alchemical tradition of Paracelsus, these three principles are sulfur, salt, and mercury.</note>.

*Ileidus*: Is the element of air; but in humans, it is the spirit which passes through all the members of the body.

*Iliaster* (or *Iliastes*, or *Iliadum*): Is the first matter of

*Iliaster primus* <note>Latin: the first Iliaster</note> is the term of life, or the life itself, or the balsam <note>original: "balsome"; a life-preserving essence within the body</note> of life in man.

*Incarnativa* <note>Latin: Incarnatives</note> are surgeons' medicines bree- <note>The word continues on the next page as "breeding," referring to medicines that promote the growth of new flesh in wounds.</note>

## L.

---

***Labor Sophia*** <note>original Latin: "Labor of Wisdom"</note> is Paradise, or another world.

***Lac Virginis*** <note>original Latin: "Virgin's Milk"</note> is Mercurial water.

***Lapis*** <note>original Latin: "Stone"</note> is used to refer to any fixed <term>stable or non-volatile</term> thing.

***Lapis Philosophorum*** <note>original Latin: "The Philosophers' Stone"</note> is the highest virtue of all earthly things, giving <term>tincture</term> <note>the power to transform or "color" other substances into gold or medicine</note>.

***Lapsus Demotinus*** is a sudden death, more dangerous than an apoplexy <note>a stroke or sudden loss of consciousness</note>.

***Laterium*** refers to the strong lye <note>alkaline liquid</note> which soap-boilers use, from which surgeons make their caustic called ***Lapis infernalis*** <note>original Latin: "Infernal Stone," a substance used to cauterize tissue</note>.

***Lato*** is copper colored with ***lapis calaminaris*** <note>calamine, a zinc ore</note> to give it a golden color <note>this process creates brass</note>.

***Laudanum*** is a compound medicine made of gold, coral, pearl, etc., by **Paracelsus** <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist</note>.

***Laudina*** is the herb **Angelica**.

***Laxa Chimolea*** is salt that has grown into stones <note>crystallized salt</note>.

***Laxativa*** <note>Laxatives</note> are medicines that work both upwards <note>as emetics to induce vomiting</note> and downwards <note>as purgatives</note>.

***Leffa*** is called the predestination of herbs <note>a Paracelsian term for the essential life force or inherent destiny of a plant</note>.

***Leffas*** is a hidden boiling vapor of the earth, by which herbs and plants grow.

***Lephante***, or ***Lephantes***, is the first kind of tartar, or bole <note>a type of medicinal earth or clay</note>, which holds a middle position between stones and clay.

***Lemures*** are spirits of the element of air, which pagans <note>original: "Heathen"</note> think to be the ghosts of departed men.

<sig>M</sig>

*Lethargyrium* in *Paracelsus* is the froth <note>original: "spume"</note> of mercury, silver, or lead, which is separated during their purification and purging.

*Lethargius* is the finder of mines.

*Limbus* in *Paracelsus* is the great and universal world, the seed and the first matter of man.

*Liquor Aquilegius* is distilled wine.

*Liquor essentialis* <note>essential liquor</note> is that fluid <note>original: "humour"</note> which is turned into flesh and blood.

*Liquor Mercurii* <note>liquor of mercury</note> is the balsam of things, in which the power <note>original: "vertue"</note> of curing or healing resides.

*Liquor Mumiae* <note>liquor of mummy; often referring to medicinal substances derived from human remains or balsamic resins</note> is the fat of a man.

*Liquor* is often used to mean oil, when the name of the substance it is derived from is added to the word "liquor."

*Liquor Mumiae de gummi* is oil of gum.

*Liquidum de resoluto* is that which is liquid by its own nature.

*Liquor herbarum* <note>liquor of herbs</note> is made of herbs that have been pounded, pressed, and digested <note>In alchemy, "digestion" refers to the process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat.</note>.

*Liquor salis* <note>liquor of salt</note> is the balsam of nature, by which the body is preserved from decay <note>original: "putrefaction"</note>.

*Locus vitae* <note>the place of life</note> is the seat of the mind or soul.

*Locustae* are the tips of tree branches while they are still tender and green.

*Lorindt* is the movement <note>original: "commotion"</note> of waters, or a harmony created by them.

*Ludus* is a medicine that cures "the stone" <note>bladder or kidney stones</note> by dissolving it.

*Lumbrici Nitri* <note>nitre worms</note> are worms found in the earth or mud, so called because of their slipperiness.

*Luna compacta* <note>compact moon</note> is <term>quicksilver</term> <note>mercury</note>.

*Luna* <note>the moon</note> is silver.

*Lustrum* is the cream of milk.

*Lunaria* is the <term>Sulphur of Nature</term>.

<summary>This page provides a glossary of alchemical and medical terms beginning with the letter "L," largely based on the works of Paracelsus. It defines various "liquors," mineral byproducts, and metaphorical terms for substances like silver and mercury.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, alchemy, chemistry, mercury, silver, medicine, liquor, litharge, Limbus, quicksilver</keywords>

## M.

*Macha* is a flying worm.

*Metaphysical Magic* <note>original: *Magia Metaphysica*</note> is an art lawful for Christians to use, by which any hidden <note>original: occult</note> secrets are discovered.

*Magic* <note>original: *Magia*</note> in general is wisdom, and it is twofold. *Natural Magic*, which is lawful, is the foundation of all true medicine <note>original: Physick</note> and the hidden <note>original: occult</note> wisdom of Nature; without this, all human reason or knowledge is mere ignorance. The other is diabolical, superstitious, and unlawful, and is called *Necromancy*, by which men attain the knowledge of things through the assistance of evil spirits.

A *Magistery* <note>original: *Magisterium*</note> is, according to Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note>, any "secret" or essence extracted out of natural things without separating them into their primary elements. These extracts are used to prepare other substances, yet only with the addition of other materials from which the extract itself is then separated.

*Magnalia* <note>Latin: "great things"</note> are the unique works of God.

*Magnesia* is commonly understood to be a marcasite <term>marcasite: in early chemistry, this referred to various metallic ores and crystallized minerals like pyrite, rather than the specific mineral known today</term>. However, that which is artificial is melted tin into which mercury <note>original: Quick-silver</note> is added, both of which are mixed into a brittle, white mass.

*Magnetic tartar* <note>original: *Magneticus tartarus*</note> is a stone—such as a kidney or bladder stone—found in a person; it is as hard as a lodestone <note>original: Loadstone</note> and spongy in texture.

<summary>This page defines alchemical and philosophical terms beginning with 'M,' distinguishing between lawful natural magic and unlawful necromancy, and explaining Paracelsian concepts like 'Magistery.'</summary>
<keywords>Macha, Metaphysical Magic, Natural Magic, Necromancy, Magistery, Paracelsus, Magnalia, Magnesia, Marcasite, Magnetic Tartar, Lodestone</keywords>

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>The previous page concludes with entries under the letter "M," and this page continues that sequence, ending with a catchword indicating the next entry will be "Mensis."</meta>

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*Magoream* is a magical medicine or secret.

*Maius noster* is our dew and the "philosophical loadstone" <note>original: *Maius noster*, literally "Our May"</note>.

*Malek* is salt.

*Mandella* is the seed of black hellebore.

*Mangonaria* is a technique used in weighing, through which heavy objects are easily made light.

*Mandibularum liquor* is oil made from jawbones <note>original: *Mandibularum liquor*</note>.

*Manna* is not only the name for a dew fallen from heaven (which is a kind of balsam or "fruit of the air"), but it is also used to describe any sweet substance extracted from any thing.

*Marcasita* is an unripe form of metal <note>original: *Marcasita*; modern: marcasite</note>, and there are as many kinds of it as there are metals.

*Marmoreus tartarus* is a stone within the human body as hard as marble <note>original: *Marmoreus tartarus*; likely referring to bladder or kidney stones</note>.

*Martath*, or *Martach*, is litharge <note>original: *Letharge*; lead monoxide, a byproduct of silver refining</note>.

*Mater metallorum* is quicksilver <note>original: *Mater metallorum*, literally "mother of metals"; mercury</note>.

*Materia saphirea* is a homogenous liquid in which there is no impurity <note>original: *Materia saphirea*, literally "sapphire matter"</note>.

*Matrices rerum* are the elements <note>original: *Matrices rerum*, literally "the wombs of things"</note>.

*Maturativa* are medicines that surgeons <note>original: *Chirurgeons*</note> use to ripen any abscess <note>original: *aposteme*</note>.

*Mechanopeotica* is the invention of waterworks to transport water through pipes to high places, or similar engineering.

*Melaones*, or *Meloes*, are flying beetles of a golden color that produce a sweet smell when rubbed; they are commonly found in meadows during the month of May.

*Melibeum* is copper.

*Melissa*, in alchemy, is understood as manna extracted from the best herbs.

<summary>This page provides a series of definitions for alchemical, medical, and technical terms starting with "M," including substances like mercury and litharge, and concepts like "matrices" or elements.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, chemistry, metallurgy, medical terminology, mercury, manna, marcasite, engineering, botany</keywords>

# A Chemical Dictionary

**The Philosophic Month** <note>original: *Mensis Philosophicus*</note> is the full time required for digestion—specifically, forty days.

**Mercury coagulated by nature** <note>original: *Mercurius à naturâ coagulatus*</note> is a solid metal.

**Crystalline Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius Crystallinus*</note> is mercury that has been brought to a crystal-like clarity through frequent sublimations <term>sublimation: the process of heating a substance into a vapor and then collecting it as a solid deposit</term>.

**Coralline Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius Corallinus*</note> is mercury that has been turned red like coral by using egg yolks and other liquids.

**Raw Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius Crudus*</note> is mercury that has not yet been separated from its ore <note>original: "Mine"</note>.

**Loose Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius laxus*</note> is Turpeth mineral <note>a yellow, poisonous chemical, historically used as a strong purgative</note>.

**Precipitated mercury of metals** <note>original: *Mercurius metallorum præcipitatus*</note> is mercury extracted from metals and then precipitated.

**Mercury of minerals** <note>original: *Mercurius mineralium*</note> is an oily substance extracted from gold and silver mines.

**Regenerated mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius regeneratus*</note> is the primary essence of mercury.

**Savage mercury** <note>original: *Mercurialis seva*</note> is the water of alum.

**Menstruum** <note>original: *Menstruum*</note> is a solvent or liquid in which any substance is dissolved or digested.

**Running metal** <note>original: *Metallum currens*</note> is quicksilver <term>quicksilver: liquid mercury</term>.

**Microcosm** <note>original: *Microcosmus*</note> is a "little world," or a human being <note>the alchemical idea that the human body reflects the structure of the entire universe</note>.

**Minium** <note>original: *Minium*</note> is the mercury—or more accurately, the Saffron of Lead <note>original: *Crocus of Lead*; a red oxide powder</note>—that has been precipitated.

**Missadam** <note>original: *Missadam*</note> is quicksilver.

**Mitigatives** <note>original: *Mitigativa*</note> are surgical medicines which relieve pain.

**Monsters** <note>original: *Monstra*</note> are animals that either do not have proper parents (and are not born of creatures like themselves), or have some physical defect or excess in their nature. For more on this, see the first book of the preceding treatise.

**Morus** <note>original: *Morus*</note> is an abscess <note>original: *aposteme*</note> that resembles a mulberry.

**Mulae** <note>original: *Mulæ*</note> are pustules caused by either heat or cold.

# A Chemical Dictionary

*Mummy* <note>original: "Mumia"</note> is not only human flesh seasoned with balsam, but any other flesh prepared in this way.
*Mummy of the Elements* <note>original: "Mumia Elementorum"</note> is the balsam of external elements.
*Overseas Mummy* <note>original: "Mumia transmarina"</note> in the writings of Paracelsus is *Manna*.
*Turned Mummy* <note>original: "Mumia versa"</note> is the liquid of mummy.
*Mucilage* <note>original: "Muscilago"</note> is a thick, viscous liquid.
*Mundificatives* <note>original: "Mundificativa"</note> are cleansing medicines.
*Mustus* is the white lime <note>original: "Calx," meaning a powdery residue or mineral deposit</note> found in urine.
*Myssadar* is mercury.
*The Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium magnum"</note> is the first matter of all things.
*Muria* is brine.

*Nacta* is an abscess <note>original: "aposteme"</note> of the breast.
*Narcotics* <note>original: "Narcotica"</note> are medicines that cause sleep.
*Nasda* or *Natta* is a hump or swelling <note>original: "bunch"</note> on the back.
*Nebulgea* is salt formed from the moisture of a cloud falling upon stones in meadows and hardened by the heat of the sun.
*Necromancy* is an unlawful art which in ancient times worked with the dead, such as when the stars were manifested through the dead. And he is truly called a *Necromancer* who can make the dead appear and can draw words and answers from them.

# A Chymicall Dictionary.

*Necrolica* are medications that ward off death and preserve life.

*Nenufareni* are spirits that dwell in the air.

*Neutha*, or *Neuta*, is a small membrane growing on the ears or eyes of infants, sometimes covering the whole body.

*Nigromancy* <note>original: "Nigromancy," a variant of necromancy.</note> is a wicked and detestable art through which devils and evil spirits allow themselves to be commanded by humans and obey them, but only to the humans' own harm.

*Nitrum* is saltpeter.

*Nitriales* are all combustible substances that lead to calcination <term>the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to change its physical or chemical state, often reducing it to powder</term>.

*Neboch* is an instrument used in necromancy.

*Nodi* are hard tumors or swellings of the joints.

*Nostoch* <note>Often called "star jelly," this is a type of cyanobacteria that swells into a gelatinous mass after rain; historically, people believed it was the remains of fallen meteors.</note> is what we call a "falling star," a kind of jelly or slime often found during the summer in fields and meadows.

*Nuba* is a type of red manna <note>In this context, a sugary or edible substance believed to fall from the sky, similar to the biblical food.</note> that falls in Ireland.

*Nymphæ*, or *Nymphidicæ*, are spiritual men or women, or physical spirits dwelling in water; such a being was Melusina <note>Melusina is a legendary figure of European folklore, usually depicted as a woman who becomes a serpent or fish from the waist down once a week while bathing.</note>.

*Nymphidica* are, metaphorically, spirits of dissolving waters in the spagyric art <term>the alchemical method of preparing medicinal extracts from plants and minerals</term>.

*Nysadir* is sal ammoniac <term>a mineral form of ammonium chloride, used in alchemy and metalworking</term>.

# O.

---

<margin>Decorative woodcut ornament</margin> **Obrizum** <note>original: *Brizum*. The initial 'O' from the section header likely completes the word.</note> is pure gold calcined <term>calcined: reduced to a powder by high heat</term> by art into a light red color.

*Ocob* is **salt ammoniac** <note>original: *Armoniack*; ammonium chloride</note>.

*Odorifera* <note>Latin: "odorous things"</note> are medicines which, by their odor—whether good or bad—expel diseases.

The **oiliness of things** <note>original: *Oleitas rerum*</note> is the sulfur found in all things.

**Burning oil** <note>original: *Oleum ardens*</note> is oil of **tartar** <term>tartar: a salt deposit from wine barrels used in many alchemical processes</term> purified <note>original: "corrected"</note> to the highest degree.

**Gold-enriched oil of vitriol** <note>original: *Oleum Vitrioli aurificatum*</note> is oil of vitriol <term>vitriol: sulfuric acid</term> that has been sweetened <note>original: "dulcified"</note> with gold by artificial means.

**Oil of Colcothar** <note>original: *Oleum Calcotharinum*</note> is red oil of **vitriol**. <term>Colcothar: the red iron oxide residue left after distilling vitriol.</term>

**Oil of scales** <note>original: *Oleum squaminum*</note> is oil of **tartar**.

The **Olympic spirit** <note>original: *Olympicus spiritus*</note> is a "star" within man that allows him to produce a shadow of himself <note>likely referring to the astral body or a spiritual projection</note>.

*Operimethiolim* is the spirit of **minerals**.

**Oppilatives** <note>original: *Oppilativa*</note> are medicines which stop flows or discharges <note>original: "fluxes"</note>.

*Opodeldoc* <note>original: *Oppodeltoch*</note> in the works of **Paracelsus** <note>a Swiss physician who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine</note> is an ointment.

*Opopopyron Laudani* is a medicine used by **Paracelsus** to stop fevers.

*Ordoleum* <note>likely *hordeolum*</note> is a small abscess <note>original: "aposteme"</note> resembling a grain of barley <note>modern: a sty</note>.

*Orexis* is a heat caused by "tartarous" <note>stony or sedimentary</note> matter.

*Organopeotica* is the art of inventing instruments of war.

The **Horizon of Eternity** <note>original: *OriZoneternitatis* / *Horizon aeternitatis*</note> is the super-celestial virtue or power inherent in things.

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*Orizeum* is gold.
*Orizeum foliatum* is leaf-gold.
*Orizeum præcipitatum* is gold brought into a cross <note>original: "Cross"; this may refer to a specific crystalline structure or a "calx" (powdered form) created through precipitation</note>, by the help of the <term>Crocus of Mercury</term> <note>a reddish powder or oxide of mercury</note>.
*Orobo* is the glass of metals <note>likely referring to the glassy slag or scoria produced during the smelting of metals</note>.
*Ossa paraleli* are a universal medicine for the gout.
*Ovum Philosophicum* is a glass vessel in the form of an egg, which philosophers <note>alchemists</note> use in their operations.
*Ozo* is arsenic.

# P.

---

*Pandalitium*, *Paneritium*, or *Passa*, is an abscess <note>original: "aposteme"</note> in the ends of the fingers, commonly called a "white-flaw" <note>now known as a whitlow or paronychia, a painful infection of the fingertip</note>.
*Panna* is a natural spot or blemish.
*Pars cum parte* <note>Latin: "part with part"</note> is a mass consisting of equal parts of gold and silver.
*Pauladadum* is a kind of <term>terra sigillata</term> <note>literally "sealed earth"; medicinal clay tablets stamped with a seal to guarantee their origin and quality</note> growing in Italy.
*Penates* are spirits of the element of fire, called familiars.
*Pentacula* <note>Pentacles</note> are signs or drawings engraved with wonderful and strange letters and images. These are hung about the neck and are said to be preservatives against evil spirits and witchcraft.
*Percipiolum* is any medicine that is approved or proven effective.
*Perdetæ* are small and yellow turnips <note>original: "rapes"</note>.

# A Chemical Dictionary

*Perdomium* is wine made from herbs.

*Periodus* is the span of a lifetime.

*Peucedamum* is English Angelica <note>likely referring to *Peucedanum officinale*, or hog's fennel, though here identified with the herb Angelica</note>.

*Phantasmata* are spirits of the woods or the desert that live in dark or hidden places.

*Phiala* is a vial or glass bottle.

*Pionitides* are natural enemies that hunt one another to death, such as the stork and frogs, etc.

*Pelicanus* is a circulatory vessel <note>original: "Pelican"; a specialized alchemical glass used for continuous distillation where vapors condense and flow back into the main liquid</note>.

*Phoenix* is the quintessence <term>the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</term> of Fire; it is also a name for the Philosopher's Stone.

*Physiognomy* is an art through which a person's nature and character are perceived by their facial features.

*Plumbum Philosophorum* <note>original: "Lead of the Philosophers"</note> is a substance extracted from antimony.

*Porosa* is St. John's Wort.

*Presagium* <note>original: "Prasagium"</note> is a notable sign or omen of a future event.

*Preservativa* are medicines that protect bodies from decay <note>original: "corruption"</note>.

*Primales* are deceptive or illusory forms that are placed before our eyes by the influence of the stars.

*Pruinum* is the primary type of tartar <term>a sediment or crust formed during wine fermentation, often used in alchemical preparations</term>.

*Pruina* is *Ignis Persicus* <note>original: "Persian Fire," a historical medical term for inflammatory skin conditions like shingles or erysipelas</note>.

*Pulpezia* is a sudden shock or physical change in a person's body caused by the influence of the stars.

*Pusta* is the processing <note>original: "digestion"</note> of decaying or corrupt matter.

*Pygmeæ* <note>original: "Pygmies"</note> are called artificial men and are sometimes considered underground spirits <note>in Paracelsian alchemy, these are often elemental spirits of the earth, like gnomes</note>.

*Pyramis* is a vessel shaped like a pyramid.

*Pyromancy* is the art of predicting the future by observing fire.

# Q.

---

*Qualitas* <note>original: "Qualitas" (Latin: quality)</note> is called a complexion, whether hot or cold, dry or moist, according to the predominance of any element.

*Quartatio*, or *Quartura* <note>quartation: a process of refining gold</note> is the highest test <note>original: "tryall"</note> of gold, performed in this way: namely <note>original: "viz." (videlicet)</note>, that nine parts of silver be mixed with one part of gold by melting them in the fire. Then, let them both be dissolved with *aqua fortis* <note>original: "Aqua fortis" (nitric acid)</note>. All the silver is turned into liquid <note>original: "water"</note>, and the gold settles to the bottom like a dark powder.

*Quintessentia* <note>quintessence</note> is a certain spiritual matter extracted physically <note>original: "corporeally"</note> out of herbs, plants, and all things that have life. It is the raising <note>original: "exalting"</note> of this matter to the highest degree of purity by separating out all impurities.

*The fifth essence of any element by itself alone* <note>original: "Quintum esse cujuslibet Elementi per se solum"</note> is a living thing <note>original: "animall"</note> produced out of that element alone.

---

# R.

---

*Ragadiæ* <note>rhagades: fissures or cracks in the skin</note> are abscesses <note>original: "apostemes"</note> of the private parts <note>original: "privities"</note>.

*Rana* <note>Latin: frog; likely referring to a "ranula" cyst</note> is an abscess <note>original: "aposteme"</note> under the tongue.

*Realgar* <note>arsenic sulfide</note> is the fume of minerals, properly speaking; but metaphorically, it refers to any corrupt <note>original: "vitious"</note> matter in a person's body from which abscesses, ulcers, or similar ailments grow.

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*<term>Rebis</term>* is the waste or excrement of the bowels. <note>In later alchemy, 'Rebis' often symbolized the 'hermaphrodite' or the union of opposites, but here it is defined in a literal, biological sense.</note>
*<term>Rebus</term>* is the final state or ultimate matter of things.
*<term>Rebisola</term>* is a secret urine-based remedy used to treat jaundice.
*<term>Rebona</term>* is the same as mummy. <note>original: "Mummie"; in early medicine, this referred to 'mumia', a substance derived from embalmed bodies or a specific medicinal preparation believed to contain life-force.</note>
*<term>Redue</term>* is a powder used to melt calcined metals and minerals. <note>Calcination is the process of reducing a metal to powder through intense heat.</note>
*<term>Regale</term>* is a cement <note>A purifying agent or paste</note> used to purge and refine gold.
*<term>Regulus</term>* is the purest metallic part of any metal or mineral, once the dross <note>original: "feces"</note> has been separated.
*<term>Relloleum</term>* is a specific virtue or power derived from a substance's nature. For example, in St. John's wort, the primary virtue is heat and healing; the other is accidental and hidden <note>original: "occult"</note>, serving as a preservative against worms and decay <note>original: "corruption"</note>.
*<term>Resina auri</term>* <note>original Latin: "resin of gold"</note> is a *<term>Crocus</term>* extracted from gold. <note>In alchemy, a 'crocus' is a deep red or yellow powder produced by calcination.</note>
*<term>Resina terrae</term>* <note>original Latin: "resin of the earth"</note> is sulfur.
*<term>Resina terrae potabilis</term>* <note>original Latin: "drinkable resin of the earth"</note> is sublimated sulfur that has been converted into a liquid, balsam, or oil.
*<term>Repercussiva</term>* are medicines used to repel or drive back a "flux" <note>An abnormal discharge of fluid in the body.</note>.
*<term>Reverberatorium</term>* is a reverberatory furnace in which material is calcined by means of a flame.
*<term>Rillus</term>* is a tool used by goldsmiths to pour melted metals into long molds; it is also called an ingot mold <note>original: "Jngat"</note>.
*<term>Rosea</term>* is the same as erysipelas <note>A bacterial skin infection characterized by a bright red rash.</note>.
*<term>Rubella</term>* is a spiritual essence that, through its dissolving power, extracts the tincture <note>The essential medicinal essence or color</note> out of physical bodies.

# S.

**S**agani are spirits of the four Elements.
*Salamandri* <note>Salamanders</note> are spirits dwelling in the fire, or fiery men.
*Saldini* are the same as *Salamandri*.
*Sal aluminosum* <note>Latin: aluminous salt</note> is salt which is extracted out of *alumen plumosum* <term>feather alum; a fibrous mineral, often asbestos or a form of sulfate</term>.
*Sal anathron* <note>Anathron salt</note> is salt extracted out of the stony moss <note>likely mineral efflorescences found on rocks</note>.
*Sal crystallinum* <note>Latin: crystalline salt</note> is salt made out of human urine <note>usually referring to phosphorus or urea crystals</note>.
*Sal Calcotharinum* <note>Calcothar salt</note> is salt which is made out of the *Colcothar* <term>red iron oxide residue</term> of *Vitriol* <term>sulfate minerals</term>.
*Sal congelatum* <note>Latin: congealed salt</note> is salt which grows in baths <note>mineral deposits from thermal springs</note>.
*Sal enixum* <note>Latin: "strained" or "exerted" salt</note> is salt dissolved <note>in later chemistry, this specifically referred to potassium bisulfate</note>.
*Sal Mercurii* <note>Latin: Salt of Mercury</note> is the spirit of the wine <note>alcohol</note> which causes drunkenness.
Saltpeter <note>potassium nitrate</note> is that which grows in cold places upon stones, congealed by the cold air.
*Sal nitrum* <note>Niter</note> is salt which is boiled out of the earth, especially "fat" <note>nitrogen-rich</note> earth, as in stables, or any place of excrements <note>animal waste</note>.
*Sal practicum* <note>Practical salt</note> is a mixture made of equal parts of saltpeter and *Sal Ammoniac* <term>ammonium chloride</term>.
*Sal scissum* <note>Split salt</note> and *alumen scissum* <note>split alum</note> are all the same thing.
*Saltz, saltz, Selenipum* <note>from German "Salz" (salt)</note> is brine.
*Saltaberzet* <note>possibly a corruption of an Arabic term</note> is the whitest *Tartar* <term>potassium bitartrate, a deposit from wine fermentation</term> of all.
*Saltabari* is *Sal Alembrot* <term>a double salt of mercury and ammonium chloride, used as a flux</term>.
*Sallena* is a kind of saltpeter.

## A Chemical Dictionary

*Sirones* are pustules or wheals, especially on the hands.

*Sirza* is an eschar <note>original: "eschara"; a dry scab or slough caused by a burn or corrosive substance.</note>.

## A Chemical Dictionary.

*Sulphur vitriolatum* <note>original: "sulphur of vitriol"</note> is sulfur extracted out of vitriol <note>term: vitriol: a sulfate of a metal, such as iron or copper</note> by common water floating on the top of it.
*Sulphur rerum* <note>original: "sulfur of things"</note> is many times taken for their quintessence <note>term: quintessence: the purest or most concentrated essence of a substance</note>.
*Supermonicum* is enigmatical <note>original: "Ænigmaticall"; meaning mysterious or symbolic</note>.
*Sylo* is the whole world.
*Sylphs* <note>original: "Sylphes"</note> are pygmies <note>In the alchemical tradition of Paracelsus, these are elemental beings associated with the air or earth</note>.
*Sylvestres* or *sylvani* <note>Latin: "forest-dwellers"</note> are airy men and airy spirits; sometimes they are taken for woodsmen that are strong, gigantic men.
*Syphita prava* is a disease called Saint Vitus's dance <note>term: St. Vitus's dance: a disorder (chorea) characterized by rapid, uncoordinated jerking movements</note>.
*Syphita stricta* is a fantastic spirit of those that walk in their sleep.

### T.

*Talc or Talcum* is a clear, shining matter, similar to pearls in color, composed of thin leaves or flakes, of which there are four kinds: namely white, yellow, red, and black.
*Tassus* is a worm.
*Tartar* <note>original: "Tartarum"</note> is a hard, salty dregs that sticks to the sides of wine vessels; also it is often taken for stones or gravel in men's bodies <note>term: stones or gravel: refers to kidney or bladder stones</note>.
*Telon* is, as it were, a dart of fire.
*Temperatum* <note>Latin: "temperate"</note> is that which exceeds not in any quality <note>referring to a balanced state of the four humors or elemental qualities</note>.
*Tenacitas glutinis* <note>Latin: "tenacity of glue"</note> is mineral resin.
*Tereniabin* is the richness of manna, or wood-honey, which is found in good quantity in the months of June, July, and August.

# A Chemical Dictionary.

*Terra auri* <note>original: "Terra auri"; Earth of gold</note> is litharge <note>lead monoxide, used in the process of separating gold and silver</note> of gold.

*Thaphneus* is a clean, purified medicine.
*Thisma* refers to mineral veins.

*Trarames* are the actions of spirits that are not seen, but only heard.
*Trifertes* are spirits that dwell in the fire.
*Trigonum* <note>original: "Trigonum"; Triangle or Trigon</note> is a fourfold transmutation of the spirits of the stars according to the number of the four elements.

*Truphat* is a hidden <note>original: "occult"</note> power <note>original: "vertue"</note> of minerals, by which they act toward the purpose for which they were ordained.
*Turba magna* <note>original: "Turba magna"; Great crowd</note> is an innumerable multitude of celestial stars and is a prophecy <note>original: "presage"</note> made by any of them.
*Turbith minerale* <note>original: "Turbith minerale"; Mineral Turpeth</note> is mercury precipitated into a sweet state <note>original: "sweetneſſe"; meaning a state where the acidic/corrosive salts have been removed</note> without any corrosive qualities.

# V.

---

*Vegetables* <note>original: *Vegetabilia*</note> are things that grow by having a root fixed in the earth, and having a stalk and branches in the air, such as herbs, plants, trees, etc.

*The degree of Venus* <note>original: *Veneris gradus*</note> is a mild and sweet nature.

*Verto* is the fourth part of a pound.

*The philosophers' filter* <note>original: *Viltrum philosophorum*</note> is an alembic <note>a chemical apparatus used for distillation</note>, but when the term is used on its own, it refers to a strainer.

*Corrected wine* <note>original: *Vinum correctum*</note> is the alcohol <note>original: Alcool</note> of wine.

*Essenced wine* <note>original: *Vinum essatum*</note> is wine impregnated with the virtues <note>medicinal or essential properties</note> of herbs or anything else.

*Greenness of salt* <note>original: *Viriditas salis*</note> is the green oil of salt.

*Diggers' twigs* <note>original: *Virgulta fossorum*</note> are certain rods which miners use to discover mines <note>likely referring to dowsing or divining rods</note>.

*Viscaleus* is mistletoe.

*Viscus* <note>birdlime or glue</note> is properly pitch boiled out of the tender tops of trees.

*Viscus of the second generation* <note>original: *Viscus secundæ generationis*</note> is blood dropping from salts.

*A vision* <note>original: *Visio*</note> is a supernatural apparition of spirits seen by men in a particular manner.

*Vitriolatum* is vitriol <note>a metal sulfate or sulfuric acid</note> which is liquid and can never be solidified <note>original: coagulated</note>.

*New vitriol* <note>original: *Vitriolum Novum*</note> is white vitriol <note>zinc sulfate</note>.

*An eating ulcer* <note>original: *Ulcus astiomenum*</note> is a cancerous <term>aposteme</term> <note>an abscess or swelling</note> that consumes the flesh where it is located.

# A Chemical Dictionary:

*Umbragines* are **Pygmies**.
*Umbratiles* are astral bodies.

*Unitas Trithemii* <note>The Unity of Trithemius, referring to Johannes Trithemius, a German abbot and occultist</note> is the reduction of the number **three** into a unity by casting away the number **two**, and it is supernatural and spagyric <term>spagyric: a term for alchemy, specifically the process of separating and recombining the components of a substance</term>.
*Uniones* are precious pearls or gems.
*Volans*, or *Unquasi* is **quicksilver** <note>mercury</note>.
*Volatile* is in general any light matter which is carried upward.

*Vulcanus* is fire <note>named after Vulcan, the Roman god of fire and metalworking</note>.

# X.

**Xenectum** <term>an amulet or preventative medicine worn on the body</term> is any outward medicine hung about the body as a preservative against the Plague.
**Xeninephidei** are spirits who delight to discover the secret or occult <note>original: "occult", meaning hidden or mystical</note> properties of nature to men, as the power to do so is granted to them.
*Xisinum* is vinegar.

# Y.

**Yrcus** <note>original: "Yrcus", derived from the Latin "hircus" for a male goat</note> is a male goat, whose blood is said to soften glass, flints, etc.
*Ysopus* is the art of alchemy used to separate the pure from the impure.

---

# Z.

**Zaidar** is mercury.
*Zaidir* is copper or verdigris <note>original: "Verdegrease", the green pigment or crust that forms on copper</note>.
*Zerus* is gold.
*Zarfa* is tin.
*Zelotum* is stony mercury.
*Zoraba* is vitriol <note>original: "Vitriall", a term for metallic sulfates often used in alchemical experiments</note>.
*Zinc* <note>original: "Zinck"</note> is a metallic marcasite <note>original: "marcasite", a term historically used to describe minerals with a metallic luster, often iron pyrites</note>, and a certain natural mixture of four immature metals, of which copper is the most apparent.
*Ziniar* is verdigris.
*Zuitter*, or *Zitter*, is a marcasite.
*Zymar*, or *Zyfar* is verdigris.

# OF THE NATURE Of Things.

---

## THE THIRD BOOK.

---

### Of the preservation of Natural things.

<margin>The cause of things.</margin>
<margin>An enmity is found in all things.</margin>
<margin>The enemies of things must be known.</margin>
**T**hat things may be preserved and kept from harm, it is necessary in the first place to know what acts as an enemy to them. This knowledge allows them to be protected from that enemy, so they are not hurt or corrupted by it—whether in their substance, virtue <note>original: "vertue"; in alchemy and early science, this refers to the inherent medicinal power or essential property of a substance</note>, power, or in any other way whatsoever. Much in this case, therefore, depends upon knowing the enemy of all natural things. For who can guard against danger when he does not know what can hurt him?

<summary>This page introduces the third book of the treatise, which focuses on the preservation of natural substances by identifying the specific "enemies" or forces that cause them to decay or lose their inherent powers.</summary>
<keywords>preservation, natural philosophy, enmity, corruption, virtue, protection, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 96 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of things, specifically focusing on the necessity of knowing opposites and the preservation of natural substances. The catchword "So" links to the beginning of the next page.</meta>

# Of the Nature of Things. Book III <note>original: "LIB. III"</note>

<margin>Death is the enemy of things.</margin>
<margin>There is a contrariety <note>original: "Contrariety"; a state of being opposite or contrary.</note> found in all things.</margin>
Truly, nobody; therefore it is necessary to know one’s enemy, for there are many sorts of enemies. Therefore, it is as necessary to know evil things as good things, for who can know what is good without knowing what is evil? Truly, nobody. Similarly, no man who was never sick can know what a blessing health is. Who knows what joy is, having never been heavy-hearted or sad? And who can rightly understand what God is, while knowing nothing of the Devil? Therefore, when God made known to us the enemy of our soul—namely <note>original: "viz."</note> the Devil—he also indicated to us the enemy of our life—namely Death—which is the enemy of our body, of our health, and the enemy of medicinal and all natural things. He also made known to us how and by what means it may be avoided. For just as there is no disease for which some remedy has not been created and found to cure and expel it, so too there is always one thing ordained against another: one water against another, one stone against another, one mineral against another, one poison against another, one metal against another, and so on in many more things, all of which it is not necessary to list here.

Now, regarding how and by what means everything is to be preserved and kept from harm, we must know that many things are to be preserved in the earth. Especially all roots remain in the earth for a long time without losing their potency <note>original: "vertue"</note> or being corrupted. In like manner, herbs, flowers, and all fruits continue in water uncorrupted and green; there are also many fruits and apples that may be preserved in water from all manner of decay <note>original: "putrefaction"</note> until the new harvest comes again.

<meta>So</meta>

<summary>The author argues that understanding "evil" or "death" is essential for appreciating "good" or "life," noting that nature provides a remedy for every affliction. He also describes methods for preserving roots, herbs, and fruits using earth and water.</summary>
<keywords>dualism, medicine, preservation, health, decay, alchemy, natural philosophy</keywords>

<margin>How to preserve flesh and blood.</margin>
In the same way, flesh and blood—which indeed rot and quickly become foul-smelling—are preserved in cold spring water. Not only that, but by adding new and fresh spring water, they may be turned into a <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: "quintessence"; in alchemy, the fifth element or the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</note> and be forever preserved from rotting and bad odors without using any balsam. This does not only preserve the flesh and blood of the dead, but also of the living; therefore, the human body may be preserved from every kind of decay and various diseases that result from putrefaction, even better than with common <term>mummy</term> <note>original: "Mummie"; refers to "mumia," a substance made from ground-up mummified remains used as a medicinal panacea</note>. Now, to ensure that blood can be preserved on its own from rotting and stinking—not just as a quintessence, but so it can preserve the blood of the living (as we have just said)—you must follow this process.

<margin>How the Arcanum of human blood is to be prepared.</margin>
Let the blood be separated from its <term>phlegm</term> <note>original: "flegm"; here referring to the clear serum or watery part of the blood that separates during settling</note>, which separates on its own and rises to the top. Gently pour this water out of the vessel, and in its place, add an equal amount of the "water of the salt of blood," which we teach how to make in our book on <term>surgery</term> <note>original: "Chirurgerie"</note>. That water mixes with the blood immediately and preserves it so that it will never rot or become foul, but will remain for many years as fresh and bright red as it was on the first day; this is truly a great wonder. But if you do not know how to make this water, or do not have it ready, then pour an equal amount of the best and most excellent balsam over it, and this will achieve the same result. Now, this blood is the "balsam of balsams" and is called the <term>Arcanum of blood</term> <note>original: "Arcanum"; a deep secret or a powerful, hidden medicinal mystery</note>. It is so wonderful and possesses such great power that it is almost beyond belief; therefore, you should keep it hidden as a great secret of <term>medicine</term> <note>original: "Physick"</note>.

<summary>This page describes alchemical methods for preserving flesh and blood using spring water or "salt of blood" to create a "quintessence" or "Arcanum." The author claims these preparations can prevent bodily decay and cure diseases more effectively than traditional medicinal mummy.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, preservation, Arcanum of blood, quintessence, medicinal mummy, salt of blood, Paracelsian medicine</keywords>

# Of the Nature of Things. Book III. <note>original: LIB. III.</note>

<margin>How metals may be preserved. What are the enemies of metals.</margin>
In the preservation of metals, their enemies must first be known so that they may be better protected from harm. The primary enemies of metals are all sharp, corroding waters <note>original: "sharp corroding Waters"; likely strong acids like nitric acid, known as aqua fortis</note>, all corrosive substances, all salts, crude sulfur, antimony, and mercury. To understand specifically how they show their hostility, it is as follows: sharp waters, corrosive things, and salts show their enmity by mortifying <note>original: "mortifie"; in alchemy, to destroy the external form or "life" of a metal</note>, dissolving, calcining, and corrupting metals until they are reduced to nothing.

<margin>How the fumes of sulfur discolor metals.</margin>
Crude sulfur shows its hostility through its fumes: for by its vapor, it strips copper of its color and redness, making it white. From white metals—such as silver, tin, lead, and iron—it removes their whiteness and turns them red or yellowish. From gold, it takes away that beautiful, pleasant yellowness and golden color, turning it black and so foul that nothing could be more repulsive.

<margin>How antimony spoils and discolors metals.</margin>
Antimony shows its hostility in this: it spoils, carries away, and preys upon all metals with which it is melted or mixed. Also, not unlike sulfur, its fumes strip metals of their true and natural color and introduce another.

<margin>How quicksilver destroys metals.</margin>
Quicksilver <note>Mercury</note> destroys metals for this reason: it enters into the metals with which it is joined and dissolves them, creating an <term>amalgam</term> <note>original: "Amalgama"; an alloy of mercury with another metal</note> of them. Therefore, its fumes—which we call common <term>mercury</term>—make all metals brittle so they cannot be <term>malleated</term> <note>original: "malleated"; hammered or shaped into sheets</note>, and it <term>calcines</term> <note>original: "calcines"; reduces a metal to a powder or oxide through heat</note> them; it also turns all red metals into a golden color.

<summary>This page identifies the chemical "enemies" that damage or corrupt metals, explaining how acids, sulfur, antimony, and mercury alter the physical properties, durability, and colors of various metallic elements.</summary>
<keywords>metallurgy, alchemy, corrosion, sulfur, antimony, mercury, chemical reactions, metal preservation, calcination</keywords>

For if common mercury <note>original: "Mercury"; often referring to quicksilver</note> even touches a bar of iron or steel, or if that bar is merely smeared over with mercurial oil, that bar will afterward break like glass and be bent. This is indeed a great secret and deserves to be kept extremely private. In the same way, the lodestone <term>a naturally magnetized piece of the mineral magnetite</term> must be kept away from mercury, for it shows the same enmity toward it as it does toward iron.

<margin>How the lodestone may be spoiled.</margin>
For any lodestone that mercury has even touched, or which has been smeared with mercurial oil, or simply placed into mercury, will never attract iron again. Let no one wonder at this, for there is a natural cause for it: mercury extracts the *spirit* of iron which was hidden in the lodestone. It is this *spirit* that draws the *spirit* of iron toward it. This occurs not only in the lodestone but in all other natural things. A foreign *spirit* within a body that is not of the same *nature* as itself always draws to itself a body that shares its own *nature*. We must understand this to be true not only of the lodestone but also of all other natural things, such as *minerals, stones, herbs, roots, humans, and animals* <note>original: "brutes"</note>.

<margin>The antipathy that exists between metals themselves.</margin>
Metals have a natural enmity and hate for one another, as seen in *lead*, which is naturally a very great enemy to *gold*. For lead breaks apart all components of *gold*; it makes it foul and weak, spoiling and destroying it even "to death" <note>In alchemy, "death" often refers to the loss of a metal's characteristic properties like luster or malleability</note>, more than any other metal.

*Tin* also hates and is an enemy to all metals; for it makes them base, brittle <note>original: "immalleable"</note>, hard, and useless if it is mixed with them in a fire or during melting.

Since, therefore, you have now heard of the enemies

<header>*Of the Nature of things.* BOOK III.</header>

Regarding metals, you must next know their preservatives, which keep them from all manner of harm or corruption, and also strengthen them in their nature and virtue, and exalt their color.

<margin>Gold is preserved in boys' urine.</margin>
First, therefore, concerning gold, you must know that it cannot be preserved better or more beautifully than in boys' urine in which sal ammoniac <note>original: "Salt Armoniack"; ammonium chloride, often used in alchemical refining</note> is dissolved, or in water of sal ammoniac alone. In these, over time, the color is so highly exalted <note>In alchemy, to "exalt" a metal meant to improve its purity, color, or properties</note> that it can be exalted no higher.

<margin>How silver is preserved.</margin>
<margin>How iron and steel may be preserved.</margin>
<margin>How copper is preserved.</margin>
Silver cannot be better preserved than if it is boiled in common water or vinegar in which tartar <note>original: "Tartar"; potassium bitartrate, a byproduct of winemaking</note> or salt has been dissolved. In this way, any old silver that has become blackened and fouled is renewed by being boiled in these waters. The best preservative for iron and steel is the lard of a castrated pig <note>original: "Barrow-hog"</note> that has not been salted; this indeed preserves iron and steel from rust if they are smeared <note>original: "fineered"</note> over with it once every month. Also, if iron is melted with fixed arsenic, it will be so renewed and fixed that, like silver, it will never contract rust. Copper may be preserved if it is only mixed with sublimed mercury, or smeared over with the oil of salt <note>original: "oyle of Salt"; historically referred to concentrated hydrochloric acid</note>; and so it will never again be grown over with verdigris <note>original: "verdegrease"; the green pigment or patina formed on copper</note>.

<margin>How lead is preserved.</margin>
Lead can in no way be better preserved than in cold earth and in a moist place, according to its nature.

<margin>How the lodestone is preserved.</margin>
The lodestone <note>original: "Load-stone"; a naturally magnetized mineral</note> is preserved best of all with the filings of iron and steel, for by this means it is never weakened, but daily strengthened.

<margin>The preservation of salts.</margin>
Now concerning the preservation of salts, and all things that are of a salty nature and may be categorized under the name of salt (of which there are

...more than a hundred types), you must know that they are to be preserved in a hot, dry place and in wooden vessels, not in glass, stone, or metal. In those materials, they dissolve and become a liquid or an <term>amalgam</term> <note>original: "Amalgama"; here referring to a softened or liquefied state caused by absorbing moisture, rather than a mercury alloy</note>, which does not happen in wood.

<margin>The preservation of liquids with oils.</margin>
Moreover, you must know how to preserve certain kinds of *waters* and *liquors* pressed out of herbs, roots, and all other fruits and vegetables, which easily collect filth and slime as if a skin were spread over them. These *waters* and *liquors* must be put into glasses that are narrow towards the top and wide below. The glasses should be filled to the top, and then a few drops of <term>olive oil</term> added so that all the water or liquor is covered. The oil will float on the top and preserve the liquor or water for a long time from filth or slime. For there is no water or liquor that will become musty or have a foul smell if it is covered with oil.

By this means, two types of water or wine liquors may also be kept separate in one vessel so that they are not mixed. This can be done with not only two sorts, but three, four, five, or more, if only the oil is put between them. They are separated by the oil as if by a wall, which will not allow them to be joined and united together; for oil and water are two opposites, and neither can be mixed with the other. Just as the oil will not allow the waters to unite, so, on the contrary, the water will not allow the oils to mix.

<margin>How clothes are preserved.</margin>
Now, to preserve cloth and garments from moths, there is no better way than with <term>mastic</term> <note>a resin from the mastic tree</note>, <term>camphor</term> <note>original: "Camphire"; a pungent aromatic substance</note>, <term>ambergris</term> <note>original: "Amber Gryse"; a waxy substance used in perfumery</note>, <term>musk</term>, and <term>civet</term> <note>a musk-like perfume obtained from the civet cat</note>. Civet is indeed the best of all, as it does not only preserve them from moths,

<page-num>26</page-num>
# Of the Nature of Things. Book III.

...but also drives away moths and all other vermin, such as fleas, lice, and so on.

<margin>How all sorts of wood may be preserved.</margin>
In addition, all types of wood—whether used in houses, bridges, ships, or anywhere else—can be preserved so that they will never rot <note>original: putrefied</note>. This works whether they are in water, underwater, out of water, in the earth, under the earth, or above the earth; and whether they are exposed to rain, wind, air, snow, or ice, in winter or summer. It also ensures that they do not become worm-eaten and that no worms will breed in them after they are cut. Now, this preservative is a great <term>Arcanum</term> <note>original: Arcanum; a deep secret or mystery of nature, often used in alchemy to refer to a powerful medicine or elixir.</note> against all kinds of rot—indeed, it is so excellent a secret that none can be compared to it. It is nothing other than:

<margin>Fixed oil of sulfur.</margin>
Oil of sulfur, the process for which is as follows: Let common yellow sulfur be powdered and put into a gourd glass <note>A flask with a round base and a long neck, used in chemical distillation.</note>. Pour enough of the strongest <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>original: Aqua fortis; literally "strong water," the historical name for nitric acid.</note> over it to cover it to a depth of three fingers. Then, draw the liquid off by distillation three or four times, and finally, distill it until it is dry. Let the sulfur that remains at the bottom—which will be a dark, dull red color <note>original: black, sad red colour</note>—be placed on marble or in a glass, and it will easily dissolve into an oil. This is a great secret for preserving wood from rot and worms. This oil so thoroughly stains <note>original: tinge</note> the wood it is coated with that it can never be washed out again. Many more things can be preserved from rot with this oil of sulfur, such as ropes, ship cables, masts, carts, fishing nets, and the snares <note>original: gins</note> used by bird-catchers and hunters. These items are often used in water or rain and would otherwise easily rot and break; the oil also preserves linen clothes and many other such things.

### BOOK III. Of the Nature of Things.
<meta>Ink bleed-through from the reverse side of the leaf is visible throughout.</meta>

<margin>Which are drinkable things, and how they are preserved.</margin>
Also, you must know how drinkable <note>original: "potable"</note> things are to be preserved, by which we understand Wine, Beer, Mead, Vinegar, and Milk. Now, if we would preserve these from harm and in their full quality <note>original: "vertue"</note>, it is very necessary that you know well what is an enemy to them, and that is <margin>Which is an enemy to them.</margin> menstruating women <note>This reflects a common Early Modern superstition that a woman's presence or touch during her period could spoil food, drink, or chemical processes.</note>: for if they handle the aforementioned things, or have anything to do with them, or even look or breathe upon them, they corrupt them. For Wine is thereby changed and becomes thick; Beer and Mead grow sour; Vinegar grows "dead" and loses its sharpness; and Milk grows sour and curdled.

This, therefore, you must know well before you come to learn how to preserve each of these in particular.

<margin>How Wine is preserved by Sulfur.</margin>
Wine is preserved chiefly by sulfur and the oil of sulfur, by which all wine may be preserved for a long time, so that it becomes neither thick nor changed in any other way.

<margin>Beer is preserved with oil of Cloves.</margin>
Beer is preserved with oil of Cloves, if some drops of it are put into it—two or three drops to every gallon—or, what is better, with the oil of the root of *Avens* <note>original: "Avens"; a plant of the genus Geum, historically used for flavoring and medicine.</note>, which preserves beer from souring.

<margin>Mead is preserved with oil of Sugar.</margin>
Mead <note>An alcoholic drink made by fermenting honey with water.</note> is preserved with oil of sugar, which must be used in the same way as the oil of cloves mentioned above.

<margin>How Vinegar is preserved.</margin>
Vinegar is preserved with oil of Ginger, which must be used in the same way as the oil of cloves mentioned above.

<margin>How Milk is preserved.</margin>
Milk is preserved with oil of Almonds made by expression <term>expression</term> <note>The process of extracting oil by pressing or squeezing.</note>, which must be used in the same way as the oil of cloves mentioned above.

<margin>Cheese is preserved with St. John's wort.</margin>
Cheese is preserved from worms with *St. John's wort*; for if the herb even touches the cheese, no worm will breed.

<margin>How honey is to be preserved.</margin>
<margin>What its chief enemy is.</margin>
Honey has no specific preservative, except that it must be kept from its enemy. Now, its chief enemy is bread; for if even a little bread made of grain <note>original: "Corne" — in this period, "corn" referred to any cereal grain like wheat or barley, rather than specifically maize.</note> is merely put or falls into it, all the honey is turned into ants <term>emmots: an archaic word for ants</term> and spoiled.
